Official Copilot help from Microsoft Support (support.microsoft.com)

2026-07-28

Microsoft Copilot Help Compendium

This compendium contains the main content of 117 articles from Microsoft’s official Copilot help on support.microsoft.com, rendered in a real browser and crawled to closure from both official hubs. It covers Microsoft Copilot – the consumer assistant in its Windows app, on the web and in Microsoft Edge, and in the iOS app – and Microsoft 365 Copilot, the workplace edition, presented as its own labeled group of sections so the two products stay distinct; there is no Alexa skill. The developer API, Copilot Studio, and administrator material are excluded per the project’s scope. Within the workplace part, articles are grouped by the app they concern (Word, Excel, PowerPoint, Outlook, Teams, Copilot Chat, and so on), an editorial grouping assigned from titles and disclosed as such. Forty-five addresses served Microsoft’s small shell page at harvest time and were skipped with log evidence; re-running the harvester retries exactly those. Site chrome and feedback prompts have been removed.

Microsoft 365 Copilot: Agents

Get started with agents in the Microsoft 365 Copilot app

[Embedded video omitted]

Agents extend what you can do with Microsoft 365 Copilot—you can customize your Copilot experience by connecting to your organization’s knowledge and data sources, as well as by automating and executing business processes. These AI-driven agents can perform a variety of tasks, working alongside you to offer suggestions, automate repetitive tasks, and provide insights to help you make informed decisions.

You can use pre-built agents, agents created by others in your organization, or create your own. You can also share a link to an agent if you want to help someone find and use a specific agent.

Agents are available in both the Web and Work tabs. #### Why use agents

  • Enhanced productivity. Agents take on mundane and repetitive tasks, freeing up your time to focus on more strategic and creative endeavors.
  • Improved accuracy and consistency. Agents, with their precise algorithms and data processing capabilities, ensure a higher degree of accuracy and consistency in your work.
  • Personalized assistance. Agents provide personalized assistance, offering suggestions and solutions that are most relevant to your tasks. #### Explore agents
  1. Start in the Microsoft 365 Copilot app.
  2. In the navigation panel, find Agents, expand the chevron to see a list of pre-installed agents—or select All agents to see everything available in the Agent Store.
  3. Select an agent. If you’re in the Agent Store, choose Add or Open.
  4. Select a prompt or type a question into the Message Copilot box. #### Share agents

You can easily get a link to an agent so you can share it with someone else.

  1. Start in the Microsoft 365 Copilot App.

  2. Look for the agent you want to share.

    • If you see the agent under Agents in the left pane, hover on it and select More (…), then select Share. The link is copied so you can share it.
      • If you don’t see the agent, select the All Agents link in the left pane, find the agent, hover on it, and select More (…), then select Share. The link is copied so you can share it. #### More ways to work with agents
  • Get more agents
  • Build your own agent
  • Uninstall agents

Share an agent in the Microsoft 365 Copilot app

Once you’ve created an agent that you like, don’t keep it to yourself. Sharing it can lead to efficiencies across your team or organization, including saving time or bringing consistency to actions and outputs. Shared agents are not editable by recipients; the agent owner retains all edit privileges. #### Share your agent

  1. Create your agent.

  2. At the end of create process you’ll see a link to the agent. Click Copy [Image: copy] to save the link to your clipboard.

    [Image: Screenshot of sharing option available on creating an agent]

  3. Select Change sharing settings.

  4. Choose how broadly you want to share the agent. For agents that use SharePoint files and folders as knowledge sources, we recommend selecting Specific users in your organization so you can specify by name or security group exactly who can access the information pulled from those sources.

    [Image: A screenshot of the options for sharing an agent]

    Important

    Agents respect user access privileges and will not include information in the response from sources they don’t have rights to.

  5. Click Save.

  6. Paste the link from your clipboard into an email, Teams chat, or other location to share the agent with others.

Not ready to share your agent yet? You can share agents any time by visiting the Microsoft 365 Copilot app, selecting your agent from the left pane, and then selecting … (More) > Share. Follow step 6 above to share.

[Image: Screenshot of where to share an agent from the left navigation.] #### More ways to work with agents

  • Get started with agents
  • Get more agents
  • Build your own agent

Uninstall agents in the Microsoft 365 Copilot app

If your agent is no longer needed, it’s easy to remove it from your agents list. Follow these steps to uninstall your agent. #### Delete your agent

  1. Browse to the Microsoft 365 Copilot app.

  2. Select your agent from the left pane, and then select … (More) > Uninstall.

  3. Select Uninstall again to confirm. Note that any saved prompts associated with this agent will remain in the Copilot Prompt Gallery.

    [Image: Screenshot of the Uninstall button for an agent.] #### More ways to work with agents

  • Get started with agents
  • Get more agents
  • Build your own agent

Microsoft 365 Copilot: Copilot Chat

About Copilot Chat responses

In this article

  • How deeply does Copilot Chat analyze prompts?
  • What to expect from Copilot Chat responses
  • Tips for better results
  • Frequently asked questions about Copilot Chat #### How deeply Copilot Chat analyzes prompts

When you enter in prompt in Copilot Chat, like a question or request, it analyzes the prompt (and any attached files for context) to figure out what you need. Based on the complexity of your request, Copilot Chat chooses the best way to respond: #### Quick answers

For simple, factual questions, Copilot Chat responds quickly with clear, concise information.

  • [Image: Icon depicting a document with sparkles] How can I quickly describe overall employee satisfaction in our company?

    • To respond to a prompt regarding company data, Copilot Chat needs access to your work data, which is only possible with a Microsoft 365 Copilot license.

  • [Image: Icon depicting a document with sparkles] Translate the following text into English (United States): [insert text].

  • [Image: Icon depicting a document with sparkles] What is [insert company]’s current stock price? #### Thoughtful analysis

For more nuanced or multi-step questions, Copilot Chat takes a moment to think through the problem and shows its reasoning step by step.

  • [Image: Icon depicting a document with sparkles] Break down the steps to launch a new product feature, including stakeholder alignment, technical implementation, and go-to-market activities.

  • [Image: Icon depicting a document with sparkles] Create an FAQ blog post based on our upcoming product releases.

    • To respond to a prompt regarding company data, Copilot Chat needs access to your work data, which is only possible with a Microsoft 365 Copilot license.

  • [Image: Icon depicting a document with sparkles] Analyze the following text and make suggestions on how to improve it: [insert text]. #### In-depth research through agents

For complex, research-heavy topics, you can use agents like Researcher and Analyst in Copilot Chat to pull together information from your work and trusted sources, organize it into a structured response, and cite sources.

  • [Image: Icon depicting a document with sparkles] @Researcher draft an executive status report on [Project].

    • To respond to a prompt regarding company data, Copilot Chat needs access to your work data, which is only possible with a Microsoft 365 Copilot license.

  • [Image: Icon depicting a document with sparkles] @Researcher help me prepare for a meeting with [Customer].

  • [Image: Icon depicting a document with sparkles] @Analyst what are the trends you see in the uploaded files? Which variables are correlated and how much? Can you visualize these correlations?

Note

A Microsoft 365 Copilot add-on license is required to use deep reasoning agents such as Researcher and Analyst. #### What to expect from Copilot Chat responses

Here’s what you might see depending on the type of question you ask Copilot Chat:

  • Type of prompt: Quick answers [Image: Icon depicting a document with sparkles] Example prompt: How many US bank holidays are there this year?; What Copilot Chat does: Gives you a direct, instant answer [Image: Icon depicting a document with sparkles] Example response: In 2025, there are 11 U.S. federal (bank) holidays observed nationwide.
  • Type of prompt: Thoughtful analysis [Image: Icon depicting a document with sparkles] Example prompt: How should I organize a presentation about my team’s performance?; What Copilot Chat does: Breaks the problem into steps and explains its thought process [Image: Icon depicting a document with sparkles] Example response: Here’s a clear way to structure your presentation so it’s engaging, easy to follow, and highlights your team’s achievements effectively: Step 1…
  • Type of prompt: Deep research or analysis [Image: Icon depicting a document with sparkles] Example prompt: @Researcher, explain current consumer demands for more sustainable products.; What Copilot Chat does: Gathers and organizes information into a detailed, and structured response [Image: Icon depicting a document with sparkles] Example response: Got it! You’re looking for insights into current consumer demands for more sustainable products. To make sure I tailor the research to your needs: …

Copilot Chat chain of thought

For complex prompts that require a great amount of analysis, Copilot Chat may show you its chain of thought, meaning the steps it took to arrive at its answer, so you can follow along and understand how it got there. For example:

  1. In Copilot Chat, @mention Researcher and enter in a prompt, like the following prompt.
    1. [Image: Icon depicting a document with sparkles] @Researcher, explain the various public opinions around [insert topic].
  2. Once Researcher in Copilot Chat completes its response, you can view the chain of thought at the beginning of its response.
  3. Select > View more to view the entire chain of thought. #### Tips for better results
  • Be specific. The more context you give, the better Copilot Chat can tailor its response.

    • Try asking Copilot Chat [Image: Icon depicting a document with sparkles] “For my upcoming performance review, show how my previous quarter’s work as contributed my team’s key results. Reference my Teams messages, emails, and meeting transcripts for details.” instead of “How well I have a performed this quarter?”

    • Note

      Only users with a Microsoft 365 Copilot license can use Copilot Chat to reference Teams messages, emails, and meeting transcripts for generating responses.

  • Attach relevant files. If your question relates to a document, spreadsheet, or email, include it in your chat prompt.
  • Ask follow-up questions. Copilot Chat remembers the context of your conversation, so you can keep refining your request without starting over.
  • Request structure. If you’d like your answer in a list, table, or summary, just ask. #### Frequently asked questions about Copilot Chat

Why did my answer take longer than usual?

  • Complex, research-heavy requests may take up to a few minutes as Copilot Chat gathers and organizes information.

  • Also, if you have standard access to Copilot Chat, your responses may take longer to process. See Standard and priority access to Copilot Chat for more information. #### How do I reset Copilot Chat context?

  • Select [Image: Teams new chat button] New chat from the left-side navigation to clear previous prompts and start fresh. #### What if I don’t see citations or chain of thought?

  • Reasoning steps appear for prompts requiring Copilot Chat to complete complex tasks and analysis. #### More ways to work with Copilot Chat

  • How Copilot Chat works with and without a Microsoft 365 Copilot license

  • Ask questions and get answers using Microsoft 365 Copilot Chat

  • Microsoft 365 Copilot pricing

Add content to Microsoft 365 Copilot Chat prompts

When you write prompts in Copilot Chat, you can add content to get more specific and detailed results from Copilot. You can also ask questions about the added content to Copilot Chat, including questions about uploaded images and images embedded in other files like PowerPoint presentations and Word documents.

Note

If you don’t have a Microsoft 365 Copilot subscription, there are some limitations to adding content and using agents in Copilot Chat. To learn about those limitations, see How Copilot Chat works with and without a Microsoft 365 Copilot license.

[Embedded video omitted] #### Add content to Copilot Chat prompts

To add content to your prompts in Copilot Chat, complete the following steps:

  1. Go to Copilot Chat and sign in with your Microsoft 365 account.

  2. In the Message Copilot compose box, select [Image: Add plus sign icon] Add and manage sources to open the menu.

  3. From the menu, select:

    1. [Image: Teams assignment attach icon] Add work content to open the work sources selection menu to find and select work content.
      1. Learn how to reference work data at Refer to specific files and more in Microsoft 365 Copilot.
    2. [Image: Browse my computer up arrow button icon] Upload images and files to open your device’s file app and select files to upload to Copilot Chat.
      1. You can upload multiple images and files to Copilot Chat from your device.
    3. [Image: OneDrive icon] Attach cloud files to open the OneDrive file picker and select files located in your OneDrive.
      1. You can select your own OneDrive files, files shared with you, and files shared in Teams meetings. #### More ways to work with Copilot Chat

Catch up on work quickly using Microsoft 365 Copilot Chat

Last updated: February 2026

Microsoft 365 Copilot can help you get summaries and catch up on things quickly. Copilot Chat can pull together quick summaries of projects, meetings, email messages, chats, and more. Use it to find out what’s due, get the latest updates on a project, or find recent communications from a colleague or manager quickly.

You can also interact with Microsoft 365 Copilot using a powerful set of voice capabilities. To learn how to use voice capabilities in Copilot, see Get started with voice features in Microsoft 365 Copilot.

Note

For Copilot to access your work data you need a Microsoft 365 Copilot add-on license. Learn more about using Copilot Chat with or without a Copilot add-on license. #### Ask Copilot Chat to help catch up on work

  1. Go to Copilot Chat and sign in to your Microsoft 365 work or school account.

  2. Ask Copilot Chat for a summary of things like a file, the latest messages from a colleague, missed meetings, or unread emails.

    1. Type / to pull up the references menu and select work sources for Copilot Chat to reference. Learn more about prompt references at Refer to specific files and more in Microsoft 365 Copilot. #### Try these prompts to catch up on your work
  • You’re working on a project and you’re looking for a key date someone shared in a document you vaguely remember. Try a prompt like this:

[Image: Icon depicting a document with sparkles] Last week, someone shared a document that defined the key deliverable dates for Project X. What were those dates?

  • You need to make sure you haven’t missed any important information from Sam, your project manager. Try a prompt like this:

[Image: Icon depicting a document with sparkles] Summarize messages from Sam over the past two weeks. Call out any important details, like deliverables, due dates, and action items for me.

  • You need key updates on a particular work project (Product Z). Try a prompt like this:

[Image: Icon depicting a document with sparkles] List the developments on Product Z that would be relevant to an executive who owns the product launch, based on collaboration over the past five business days.

  • You’re back from vacation and need a recap of some meetings you missed. Try a prompt like this:

[Image: Icon depicting a document with sparkles] Recap / Customer Success Team Sync.

If the meeting you’re recapping has recorded visuals, Copilot also creates a 20-40 second video recap of the meeting, highlighting moments from the recording. #### More ways to work with Copilot Chat

Control and review sources of Microsoft 365 Copilot Chat’s responses

Do you wonder where Microsoft 365 Copilot Chat gets information from when generating responses? There are many sources Copilot Chat can draw upon to create responses. Moreover, Copilot gives you control over its knowledge sources.

Copilot can retrieve information from:

  • Web searches
    • Your IT admin must have a Web search turned on for you.
    • Learn more about web search in Copilot Chat at Understanding web search in Microsoft 365 Copilot Chat.
  • Work data and connections
    • Work data is only accessible for people with a Microsoft 365 Copilot subscription.
    • Learn more about work data access at What information does Copilot use to answer my prompt?
  • Copilot Memory
    • Learn more about memory personalization at Personalize what Microsoft 365 Copilot remembers. #### Turn off and on Work IQ for referencing work data

The Work IQ toggle controls whether Copilot Chat can reference work data like emails, Teams messages, files, and more. Learn more about Work IQ at What information does Copilot use to answer my prompt?. #### Control data sources in Copilot Chat

When you’re drafting a prompt in Copilot Chat, control the sources Copilot can access in the Change data sources menu.

  1. Go to Copilot Chat and sign in with your Microsoft 365 work or school account.
  2. In the Message Copilot compose box, select the [Image: Add plus sign icon] Add and manage sources button.
  3. At the bottom of the menu, select [Image: Two dots connected with line to add sources to Copilot Chat prompt] Change data sources.
  4. In the Change data sources menu, use the toggles to turn on and off the sources you want Copilot to reference when generating a response.
    1. For example, you can turn off Viva Engage to prevent Copilot from using Viva Engage data to respond to your prompt.
  5. Select the Turn off all button at the top of the data sources menu to turn off all data sources except for web search.
  6. Finish writing your prompt and select [Image: Arrow icon to send message to Copilot Chat] Send. #### Review sources in Copilot Chat

Once Copilot Chat responds to your prompt, you have complete visibility into the sources Copilot referenced when generating a response. #### Review inline citations

Copilot Chat responses include inline citations of its sources next to the information it generated.

  1. In a Copilot Chat response, find an inline citation and hover your cursor over it.
  2. When hovering of the inline citation, you can:
    1. Select the source file to open it in a side pane in Copilot Chat.
    2. Select Ask Copilot to create a new prompt referencing that source specifically.
    3. Select … More actions for tasks, like summarizing files, drafting a response to emails, or seeing who contributed to the file. #### Review the Sources list

You can also view a complete list of all Copilot’s sources by completing the following steps:

  1. At the bottom of a Copilot Chat response, select the Sources button.
  2. In the Sources side pane, scroll through the entire list of Copilot’s sources.
  3. Select a source, and another side pane opens with the source file.
  4. At the top of the file’s pane, select the [Image: Box with an arrow icon to open a source in an app] Open in … button to open the file in its related app.
    1. For example, select Open in Outlook to open the referenced email in Outlook. #### More ways to work with Copilot Chat

Create content using Microsoft 365 Copilot Chat

Last updated: February 2026

Microsoft 365 Copilot empowers you to brainstorm ideas and generate new content, including storyboards, speech scripts, meeting agendas, images, charts, and presentations. Reference source materials in your prompt to give Copilot detailed guidance on what it should create.

You can also interact with Microsoft 365 Copilot using a powerful set of voice capabilities. To learn how to use voice capabilities in Copilot, see Get started with voice features in Microsoft 365 Copilot. #### Create content using Copilot Chat

  1. Open Copilot Chat and sign in with your work or school account.
  2. In the Message Copilot compose box, enter your prompt asking Copilot to create content for you.
  3. When you’re done, select [Image: Arrow icon to send message to Copilot Chat] Send. #### Try these prompts to create content using Copilot Chat

These example prompts cover some of things you can create with Copilot Chat. You can copy and paste these prompts or adjust them to fit your needs.

[Image: Icon depicting a document with sparkles] Create a template for a letter announcing an important promotion and put it in Word document.

[Image: Icon depicting a document with sparkles] Create a PowerPoint presentation targeted to my organization’s leaders to understand the customer sentiment data in /file.xlsx.

[Image: Icon depicting a document with sparkles] Create an Excel spreadsheet of fake sales data for North, South, East, and West regions.

[Image: Icon depicting a document with sparkles] Draft an email to my team thanking them for their hard work on Project X.

  • Select Edit in Outlook to send the email draft to Outlook and continue editing the email in Outlook.

[Image: Icon depicting a document with sparkles] Generate an image showing presentation dos and don’ts with side-by-side examples of acceptable and unacceptable practices.

  • Once the image is generated, use the in-app editing tools to adjust the image .

[Image: Icon depicting a document with sparkles] Create an interactive, in-memory-only app to evaluate vendors after each project. The app should allow users to rate vendors on criteria such as delivery time, quality, and communication. Each evaluation should be associated with a specific project and reviewer.

  • Learn more about building lightweight apps with Microsoft 365 Copilot at Build lightweight apps within Microsoft 365 Copilot Pages.

[Image: Icon depicting a document with sparkles] Give me a chart that breaks down the 10 lowest performing stores in North America using /file.xlsx.

[Image: Icon depicting a document with sparkles] I need to explain Concept A to a CEO. I need to include an explanation of what it is, how it works, and a value proposition associated with it. Create a script I could use. Also include two analogies that can help a non-technical person understand it.

[Image: Icon depicting a document with sparkles] Give me 10 potential name ideas for Product B that capture the attention of millennials.

Tip

You can only use the / key to reference a specific file if you have a Microsoft 365 Copilot subscription. Learn more about using Copilot Chat with or without a Copilot subscription. #### More ways to work with Copilot Chat

Dictate messages to Microsoft 365 Copilot Chat

Microsoft 365 Copilot Chat allows you to dictate messages for easier notetaking, faster transcriptions, and better accessibility.

  • Hands-free productivity: Compose messages, documents, or notes using your voice, reducing the need for typing.
  • Quick idea capture: Dictate thoughts in real time, ideal for meetings or brainstorming sessions.
  • Accessibility: Supports inclusive work environments by enabling voice-based input for users with different needs. #### Start a dictated message with Copilot Chat

Note

The first time you access any of Copilot voice features, you may be prompted to grant Copilot access to your microphone. You can manage this setting in your browser or device settings. Minimize background noise for accuracy.

  1. Go to Microsoft 365 Copilot or open the Microsoft 365 Copilot app on your desktop, then sign in with your work or school account.

  2. In the chat prompt box, select [Image: Teams microphone icon] Start dictation to start dictating.

  3. Speak clearly and naturally, and Copilot will transcribe your speech into text in real time.

  4. To stop dictation, select [Image: Microphone to stop dictation] Stop dictation. At that point, Copilot stops listening.

    1. Always review transcribed or generated content for accuracy as AI may occasionally misinterpret speech or produce errors.

Tip

Use punctuation commands like “period,” “comma,” or “new line” for better formatting. #### More ways to work with Copilot Chat

Get started with voice features in Microsoft 365 Copilot

Have Microsoft 365 Copilot read aloud to you

Frequently asked questions about voice features in Microsoft 365 Copilot

Frequently asked questions about Microsoft 365 Copilot Chat

Applies To

Outlook for Microsoft 365 Microsoft Teams #### What can Copilot do?

Microsoft 365 Copilot is your AI assistant for work that can help you with tasks like brainstorming, writing, coding, and searching. It provides intelligent suggestions and automates repetitive tasks to make your work easier. You can find Copilot Chat in the apps you use every day, like Microsoft 365, Teams, Outlook, and Edge. Copilot helps you stay connected, be creative, and more productive. #### How does Copilot work?

Copilot uses a large language model that mimics natural language based on patterns from large amounts of training data. The model is optimized for conversation by using Reinforcement Learning with Human Feedback (RLHF)—a method that uses human demonstrations to guide the model toward a desired behavior.

When you submit a text prompt to Copilot, the model generates a response by making suggestions about what text should come next in a string of words. The model is based on a domain-specific language (DSL) that allows you to specify what kind of information you want to search and synthesize from either your Microsoft 365 data or from the web. It’s important to keep in mind that the system was designed to mimic natural human communication, but the output may be inaccurate or out of date. #### What’s the difference between the work and web modes in Copilot Chat?

With a Microsoft 365 Copilot license, the Work IQ toggle at the top left of the Copilot user interface allows you to switch between grounding your prompts in work data and web data when On or just web data when Off. Both options provide generative AI services, but they serve different purposes to help you be more productive.

  • When Work IQ is on, Copilot Chat responses are grounded in work data available to you. Using the Microsoft Graph, Copilot can help you access organizational resources or content, such as documents in OneDrive, emails, or other data. This is where you can manage and complete tasks related to your work. You can draft emails, create documents, manage your calendar, and collaborate with colleagues directly from this mode.
  • When Work IQ is off, Copilot Chat is grounded in data from the public web in the Bing search index. This mode combines the power of generative AI with up-to-date information from the web. It’s useful for conducting research, finding resources, and staying updated with the latest news and trends.

These modes are designed to streamline your workflow and make it easier to switch between work-based tasks and web-based information sources. Both modes offer enterprise-grade security, privacy, and compliance. #### How do I best use Copilot Chat?

Use natural language to ask Copilot questions or give instructions.

When Work IQ is on, try asking questions about your organization, for help generating content, or for a summary of a topic, document, or chat conversation. The more specific your prompt, the better the results.

Here are some work-based example prompts to help you get started:

  • Summarize unread emails from John.
  • Draft a message to my team with action items from my last meeting.
  • What’s our vacation policy?
  • Who am I meeting with tomorrow?

When Work IQ is off, get quick summaries about specific topics, analyze or compare products to make informed business decisions, create images, draft content, and learn new skills.

Here are some web-based example prompts you might want to try:

  • Give me a summary of recent news about [product].
  • Help me learn about [topic].
  • How do I set achievable goals at work?
  • How does our internal forecast compare with EPS growth in the top US public CPG companies? #### Can I use Copilot Pages when Work IQ is off?

Copilot Pages is available when Work IQ is on or off. With a Microsoft 365 Copilot or Microsoft 365 license, access Copilot Pages by:

  • Select Edit in page at the bottom of a response.
  • The response opens in a new page beside the chat thread.
  • You can then edit, add information, and share it.

Tip

Access previous pages in recent chats or by selecting Library from the Microsoft 365 Copilot app nav menu. #### Can I use Copilot Chat with my work account and personal account at the same time?

No, you cannot use Copilot with your personal and work account at the same time. To sign in with your personal or work account, sign in at m365copilot.com.

Tip

Microsoft Edge supports creating both work and personal profiles and makes it easy to have both open at the same time. See Sign in and create multiple profiles in Microsoft Edge. #### How is Copilot evaluated?

To ensure quality, Copilot is given test questions, and its responses are evaluated based on criteria such as accuracy, relevance, tone, and intelligence. Those evaluation scores are then used to improve the model. It’s important to keep in mind that the system was designed to mimic natural human communication, but the output may be inaccurate, incorrect, or out of date.

Providing feedback via the Thumbs Up and Thumbs Down icons aids in teaching Copilot which responses are and aren’t helpful to you as a user. We use this feedback to improve Copilot, just like we use customer feedback to improve other Microsoft 365 services and Microsoft 365 apps. We don’t use this feedback to train the foundation models used by Copilot.

Customers can manage feedback through admin controls. For more information, see Manage Microsoft feedback for your organization. #### How does Microsoft 365 Copilot handle and protect my sensitive data and information?

Microsoft 365 Copilot presents only the data that each individual can access using the same underlying controls for data access used in other Microsoft 365 services. The permissions model within your Microsoft 365 tenant helps ensure that data won’t leak between users and groups. For more information on how Microsoft protects your data, see Microsoft Privacy Statement.

Prompts and responses aren’t used to train foundation models. #### Where can I learn more about privacy with Copilot?

Microsoft 365 Copilot is built on the Microsoft comprehensive approach to security, compliance, and privacy.

For more information, refer to the following:

  • If you’re using Microsoft 365 Copilot in your organization (with your work or school account), see Data, Privacy, and Security for Microsoft 365 Copilot.
  • If you’re using Microsoft 365 apps at home (with your personal Microsoft account), see Copilot in Microsoft 365 apps for home: your data and privacy. #### How accurate are Microsoft 365 Copilot responses?

Microsoft 365 Copilot leverages the latest information from the web and your available work data to generate responses based on the modes you use. While AI generates these responses and strives for accuracy, it can occasionally make mistakes. To help you verify the information, Copilot provides references for where the information originated.

If you find an answer is incorrect or if you encounter harmful or inappropriate content, provide feedback by selecting the Thumbs Down icon and describing the issue in detail. #### Where can I learn more about the Microsoft commitment to responsible AI?

As AI is poised to transform our lives, we must collectively define new rules, norms, and practices for the use and impact of this technology. Microsoft has been on a Responsible AI journey since 2017, when we defined our principles and approach to ensuring this technology is used in a way that is driven by ethical principles that put people first. Read more about Microsoft’s framework for building AI systems responsibly, the ethical principles that guide us, and the tooling and capabilities we’ve created to help customers use AI responsibly. #### What is the chat capability in Microsoft 365 Copilot and how does it differ from Microsoft 365 apps?

Microsoft 365 Copilot in Word and other apps can help you carry out specific goals and tasks. For example, Copilot in Word can help you create drafts, summarize a document, and more.

Copilot Chat within Microsoft 365 Copilot helps you ground your prompts in work and web data. #### What is the Try these prompts section?

You can write your own prompts to get data summaries, brainstorm new content, and get quick answers (for example, about the location of files). You can add new info and detail to the prompt and try again if you want more or less than the results Copilot provides.

You can learn a lot more about prompts by going to the prompting toolkit. #### Where are my prompts stored in Copilot?

When you enter prompts using Copilot, the information contained in the prompt, the data retrieved, and the generated response remains within your Microsoft 365 tenant, in keeping with our current privacy, security, and compliance commitments.

Actions that you perform on a prompt after sending it to Copilot, like prompt saving, stores a new version of that prompt for your individual use.

You can access your saved prompts in Copilot by selecting the … Prompt Lab below the Message Copilot prompt box. To learn how to save, delete, or manage your saved prompts, see How to save prompts.

All this data is stored compliantly and adheres to Microsoft 365’s general privacy, security, and compliance commitments. For more information, see Data, Privacy, and Security for Microsoft 365 Copilot. #### How are the titles in my Copilot chat history created?

When you start a new Copilot chat, Copilot automatically generates a chat title to display in your chat history list. This is to make it easier to find chats you’ve had with Copilot in the past.

You can change the generated title by selecting the … More button and replacing the generated title with one of your own. #### What languages are supported?

Copilot supports a wide range of languages. For the latest information on languages that have been tested and validated for all Copilot apps, see Supported languages for Microsoft 365 Copilot.

You can also try other languages with Copilot Chat though results may vary. If you try another language, use the Thumbs Up or Thumbs Down feedback controls to let us know how your experience was. #### How does Copilot use my uploaded images in image generation?

When you upload an image for use in image generation, Copilot processes it using an AI model to help fulfill your request. This includes processing of the full image, including visual elements such as objects, background, and style contained therein. If the image includes people, this analysis may include their physical and facial characteristics. For example, depending on your request, the model may assess how many individuals are in the image, group individuals, alter the background, or add new elements. While the model may, in certain circumstances, spot well-known public figures, the AI model cannot otherwise identify unique individuals.

Enterprise customers are responsible for ensuring they have all appropriate rights and necessary permissions to use any uploaded images, including the permission of all individuals provided. #### How can I verify if an image was edited or generated with AI in Microsoft 365 Copilot?

For AI generated images created within Microsoft 365 Copilot, we have implemented content credentials, provenance based on the C2PA standard, to help people identify whether images were edited or generated with AI. Provenance metadata can be viewed on the Content Credentials site.

Get started with Microsoft 365 Copilot Chat

Microsoft 365 Copilot Chat is at the center of Microsoft 365 Copilot. When grounded in web data, you can ask Copilot questions, help draft content, and summarize files. If you have the Microsoft 365 Copilot subscription, you can switch Copilot to incorporate your work content, such as chats, email, and files.

You can also interact with Microsoft 365 Copilot using a powerful set of voice capabilities. To learn how to use voice capabilities in Copilot, see Get started with voice features in Microsoft 365 Copilot. #### How to use Copilot Chat

  1. Go to Copilot Chat and sign in with your Microsoft 365 work or school account.

  2. In Copilot Chat, write a prompt in the Message Copilot compose box.

    • Select [Image: Add plus sign icon] Add and manage sources if you want to:
      • Add content to Microsoft 365 Copilot Chat prompts
      • Add AI capabilities and agents to your Microsoft 365 Copilot Chat prompts
      • Control data sources Copilot Chat can reference
    • If you have a Copilot subscription, you can type a / and begin typing the people, files, meetings, or emails you wish to use as references. Learn more at Refer to specific files and more in Copilot.
    • You can also dictate your prompt to Copilot Chat. Select [Image: Teams microphone icon] Start dictation to speak your prompt to Copilot Chat. Learn more at Get started with voice features in Microsoft 365 Copilot.

  3. When you’re satisfied with your prompt, select [Image: Arrow icon to send message to Copilot Chat] Send.

    • Copilot Chat begins generating a response to your prompt.

To collaborate with your team, save your chats to a Copilot Page. Learn more at Get started with Copilot Pages. #### Try these prompts in Copilot Chat

How Copilot Chat works in Microsoft 365 apps

Last updated: April 2026

Copilot Chat is available side-by-side in some Microsoft 365 apps, like Word, Excel, PowerPoint, and OneNote. Read this article to learn how Copilot Chat works in various Microsoft 365 apps.

Tip

  • All users with an eligible Microsoft 365 subscription, including all work or school account users can use Copilot Chat in Outlook.
  • Whether you have a Copilot add-on license or not will determine what Copilot capabilities you have in the Microsoft 365 apps. To use Copilot Chat in the apps, you need to be signed in with your work or school account. #### Outlook

All subscribers have access to Copilot in Outlook. With Copilot Chat in Outlook, you can interact naturally with your inbox and calendar to get more done in less time.

What you can do with Copilot Chat in Outlook depends on whether or not your admin assigned a Microsoft 365 Copilot add-on license to your work or school account.

Copilot Chat without an add-on license: If you don’t have a Microsoft 365 Copilot add-on license with your work or school account, you can use Copilot Chat in Outlook to ask questions about your inbox, calendar, meetings, and other limited data. Here are examples of the types of prompts you can use:

[Image: Icon depicting a document with sparkles] Summarize my emails from the last week related to [project or customer name].

[Image: Icon depicting a document with sparkles] When is my next 1:1 meeting with /name?

[Image: Icon depicting a document with sparkles] Draft an email update summarizing the customer escalations from this week.

Copilot Chat with an add-on license: If you have a Microsoft 365 Copilot add-on license with your work or school account, you’ll be able to use Copilot Chat in Outlook to interact with your inbox, calendar, meetings, chats, and enterprise data, using prompts such as:

[Image: Icon depicting a document with sparkles] Which unread emails are most important? Summarize them, synthesize any related context from my chats and documents, and prioritize the order in which I should respond.

[Image: Icon depicting a document with sparkles] Tell me when my next meeting with my skip-level manager is scheduled, help me draft an agenda based on my current projects, and create a first draft of a pre-read.

[Image: Icon depicting a document with sparkles] Create a welcome email to a new hire, including details on what they should do in their first four weeks, an org chart for the team including details on each person’s role and their current projects, and related documents that they should review.

[Image: Screenshot of Outlook showing Copilot Chat pane]

How Copilot Chat works with and without a Microsoft 365 Copilot license

Copilot Chat is available to Microsoft business users. The features you can use depend on whether your admin has assigned you a Microsoft 365 Copilot add-on license.

[Embedded video omitted]

Note

To use Copilot Chat you need to sign in with your Microsoft 365 account and have a qualifying Microsoft 365 business or enterprise subscription. #### Copilot Chat with or without an add-on license

  • Feature: AI model access; Without a Copilot license (Copilot Chat only): Standard access; With a Copilot license Microsoft 365 Copilot: Priority access
  • Feature: Web data reasoning; Without a Copilot license (Copilot Chat only): Yes; With a Copilot license Microsoft 365 Copilot: Yes
  • Feature: Work data reasoning (Work IQ); Without a Copilot license (Copilot Chat only): Limited (uploaded files); With a Copilot license Microsoft 365 Copilot: Full (meetings, emails, chats, files, and more)
  • Feature: Access to agents; Without a Copilot license (Copilot Chat only): Limited (no advanced agents); With a Copilot license Microsoft 365 Copilot: Extensive use (including advanced agents like Researcher, Analyst, and custom agents)
  • Feature: Security; Without a Copilot license (Copilot Chat only): Enterprise grade; With a Copilot license Microsoft 365 Copilot: Enterprise grade
  • Feature: Copilot studio access; Without a Copilot license (Copilot Chat only): Pay-as-you-go; With a Copilot license Microsoft 365 Copilot: Included
  • Feature: Priority; Without a Copilot license (Copilot Chat only): Standard (may be limited during peak times); With a Copilot license Microsoft 365 Copilot: Priority (faster, more reliable)
  • Feature: Copilot in Outlook; Without a Copilot license (Copilot Chat only): Included; With a Copilot license Microsoft 365 Copilot: Included
  • Feature: Copilot in Microsoft 365 apps; Without a Copilot license (Copilot Chat only): Depends on your label; With a Copilot license Microsoft 365 Copilot: Included

For additional information about what you have access to with Copilot sign into Copilot.microsoft.com check what label is assigned to you.

A label of Copilot Chat (Basic), M365 Copilot (Basic), or M365 Copilot (Premium) appears under your name.

This label indicates where you can access Copilot directly. Learn more about labels and what they mean in What Copilot license do I have. #### Copilot help for IT admins

If you’re an admin and want to learn more about setting up Copilot Chat in your organization, see the following:

  • Overview of Microsoft 365 Copilot Chat
  • Microsoft 365 Copilot overview
  • Data, privacy, and security for web search in Microsoft 365 Copilot and Microsoft 365 Copilot Chat

How Microsoft 365 Copilot Chat history works

Want to go back to that chat you had with Microsoft 365 Copilot Chat earlier in the week? Chat history is here for you. #### How do I find my chat history?

You’ll find your chat history in the Microsoft 365 Copilot app.

  1. Go to Copilot Chat and sign in with your Microsoft 365 account.

  2. In Copilot Chat, find the Chats list on the left-side navigation.

  3. Select a chat conversation to revisit.

    1. Read through the chat or continue the chat with Copilot Chat.

  4. Next to the chat’s title, select … More to:

    1. [Image: Rename chat icon] Rename: Allows you to rename the conversation.
    2. [Image: Outline of a book with plus sign] Move to notebook: Adds the chat conversation to Copilot Notebooks .
    3. [Image: Icon used on button in Journal that deletes content] Delete: Deletes the conversation from chat history.

Tip

If you’d like to clear all of your Copilot activity history, see Delete your Microsoft 365 Copilot activity history. #### How do I turn off Copilot personalization based on chat history?

Copilot Memory automatically collects key details from your previous chat conversations and remembers it for future conversations.

If you don’t want Copilot Memory to use your previous chats to respond to future prompts, complete the following steps:

  1. Go to Copilot Chat and sign in with your Microsoft 365 account.
  2. At the top right of Copilot Chat, select … Settings and more and select [Image: Machine cogs to access Copilot settings] Chat settings from the menu.
  3. In the Chat settings window, select [Image: People with machine cogs for Copilot personalization settings] Personalization from the left-side menu.
  4. In the Chat history tile, move the toggle to the Off position.

Caution

  • Turning off Chat history personalization deletes any inferences Copilot made from previous chats.
  • This data is removed from the Copilot system after 30 days, ensuring your privacy and control over what’s remembered.
  • If the toggle is turned on within the 30-day grace period, Copilot overrides the deletion so that conversation data isn’t lost. #### How does Copilot use my chat history?

Copilot may use context from previous chat conversations to improve future responses by making inferences about what is important to remember. For example, if you once mentioned you’re planning a business trip to Japan, it might suggest travel tips when you ask, “What should I pack?”

Copilot doesn’t remember every detail from your previous chats, only what’s useful for personalizing its responses to your questions and tasks.

If there’s a chat conversation you don’t want Copilot to reference, you should delete that conversation. #### More ways to work with Copilot Chat

  • Get started with personalizing what Microsoft 365 Copilot remember
  • Manage Copilot Memory in Microsoft 365 Copilot
  • Customize how Microsoft 365 Copilot responds to you
  • Ask questions and get answers using Microsoft 365 Copilot Chat
  • Frequently asked questions about Microsoft 365 Copilot Chat

How web search works in Microsoft 365 Copilot Chat and agents

Copilot Chat and Agents can use web search to improve the quality of their responses by referencing the latest publicly available information.

This helps Copilot provide more complete, current, and relevant answers—especially when your prompt involves topics that go beyond your work content.

Copilot generates a short query based on your prompt and sends it to Bing. The results are used to enhance your response with relevant content from the web.

Note

The web search queries generated by Copilot and sent to Bing are subject to the Microsoft Services Agreement between each user and Microsoft, together with the Microsoft Privacy Statement. In the event of conflict with respect to the use of Bing with Microsoft 365 Copilot, Microsoft 365 Copilot Chat, or Microsoft 365 Agents, the Product Terms supersede the Microsoft Services Agreement and the Microsoft Privacy Statement. Learn more. #### Why web search matters

When web search is on, Copilot Chat and Agents generate a short Bing query based on your prompt. This allows Copilot to reason more effectively over your prompt by incorporating timely, relevant information from the web.

This is especially helpful when:

  • You’re exploring specialized or emerging topics
  • You need current industry trends or market data
  • You’re planning travel or events
  • You’re conducting competitive analysis or research #### Search the web with prompts like

[Image: Icon depicting a document with sparkles] What are the latest trends in hybrid work?

[Image: Icon depicting a document with sparkles] Summarize today’s news in the energy sector

[Image: Icon depicting a document with sparkles] Help me learn about quantum computing

[Image: Icon depicting a document with sparkles] Summarize today’s news in the energy sector

[Image: Icon depicting a document with sparkles] Compare product strategies between product [name] and product [name]

Information sources for Web search

When Copilot uses web search, you’ll see a Sources button below your response.

Select the button to view the exact query Copilot sent to Bing and the sources it used. This helps you understand how Copilot interpreted your prompt and builds trust in the results. #### How Web search works

  • Depending on if you have a Microsoft 365 Copilot license or not, your web search experience may have standard access or priority access to Copilot Chat services. To learn more about standard and priority access to features in Microsoft 365 Copilot, see Standard and priority access to Copilot Chat.
  • Copilot generates a Bing search query using a few words from your prompt or any file you’ve uploaded.
  • The full prompt or file is not used as the query unless it’s already very short (for example, “New York City weather”).
  • If your organization allows web search, you’ll be able to use it without taking additional action. #### Turn on Web search for Copilot Chat and Agents

Your organization’s policies determine whether Copilot uses web search. If your organization has turned on Copilot, you can control whether Copilot uses web information too when Work IQ is turned on.

To turn web search on or off:

  1. In Copilot Chat, select the … Settings and more menu in the top-right corner.
  2. From the menu select [Image: Machine cogs to access Copilot settings] Chat settings.
  3. From the settings menu, select [Image: People with machine cogs for Copilot personalization settings] Personalization.
  4. Scroll to the bottom of the Personalization settings page and expand the Advanced section.
  5. Use the Web search toggle to turn web search on or off.

If web search is turned off by your organization, Copilot still responds to your prompts but without the benefit of current web information.

You may see a message like:

  • “Web search is off. Responses won’t include web insights, which may affect relevance. Your organization controls this setting.”

To turn on web search if your organization has turned it off, reach out to your administrator.

Tip

For admins who want more information about using web search in their organization, see Manage web search queries in chat and agents.

Manage automatic replies with Microsoft 365 Copilot Chat

Use Microsoft 365 Copilot Chat to quickly set up, update, turn off, or draft your automatic replies when you’re planning your out of office, without having to navigate through multiple settings.

Note

  • To use Copilot to set up and manage automatic replies, you need a Microsoft 365 subscription and a Microsoft 365 Copilot subscription.
  • Currently, automatic replies with Copilot are available only in English, with support for additional languages coming later.
  • Supported platforms include the Microsoft 365 Copilot app on web, Windows, Mac, iOS, and Android devices #### Set up or manage your automatic replies with Copilot

  1. Sign in to Copilot Chat using your Microsoft 365 account.

  2. In Copilot Chat, enter in a prompt like:

    [Image: Icon depicting a document with sparkles] Set up automatic replies for tomorrow. |

  3. Review the drafted automatic reply that Copilot prepares based on your previous out-of-office style and the details you provide.

  4. Confirm the draft message to set up your automatic reply.

  5. To make changes later, use prompts like:

  • Example prompts: [Image: Icon depicting a document with sparkles] Update my automatic reply.
  • Example prompts: [Image: Icon depicting a document with sparkles] Turn off my automatic replies.

How Copilot personalizes your automatic reply

When drafting your automatic reply, Copilot uses:

  • The style and tone of your previous automatic replies.

  • The context you share in your prompt or the chat, like date ranges, backup people, and message details.

  • If you have an email open in Copilot Chat, Copilot can reference the email details.

    • For example, if you have a vacation approval email open, Copilot automatically uses the dates from the email content to help draft the automatic reply. #### Set up automatic replies in Copilot Chat with these prompts

  • Example prompts: [Image: Icon depicting a document with sparkles] Set up automatic replies for next week while I’m on vacation.

  • Example prompts: [Image: Icon depicting a document with sparkles] Draft an out-of-office message letting people know I’ll be offline this afternoon.

  • Example prompts: [Image: Icon depicting a document with sparkles] Update my automatic reply to include my colleague’s contact information while I’m out.

  • Example prompts: [Image: Icon depicting a document with sparkles] Turn off my automatic replies starting Monday morning.

  • Example prompts: [Image: Icon depicting a document with sparkles] Rewrite my automatic reply to sound more formal.

  • You can also set up automatic replies using Copilot in Outlook and other Microsoft 365 apps. Learn how to use Copilot in Outlook for automatic replies at Manage automatic replies with Microsoft 365 Copilot in Outlook. #### More ways to work with Copilot Chat

Standard versus priority access to features in Microsoft 365 Copilot Chat

Standard and priority access to features in Copilot Chat

The level of access to some Copilot capabilities differs based on whether a user has a Microsoft 365 Copilot license or not. Learn how to check what license you have.

  • Priority access: Users with a Microsoft 365 Copilot license receive priority access to Copilot capabilities, which is designed to provide faster response times and more consistent availability compared to standard access, including during peak usage periods, subject to service availability.
  • Standard access: Users without a Microsoft 365 Copilot license have standard access to Copilot capabilities, which is subject to service capacity and may vary throughout the day. See more details under the following Notes.

These capabilities include but are not limited to file upload, image generation, voice features*, models, and advanced reasoning. Availability of these features may change over time and can vary based on service conditions.

Note

  • *Voice features in Copilot are currently only available to users with priority access to Microsoft 365 Copilot.

  • During periods of high demand, access to Copilot capabilities with standard access may be temporarily restricted. This isn’t an error or product malfunction but a way to help ensure a reliable Copilot experience for the most users.

  • Quality and performance of some capabilities with standard access may vary throughout the day depending on the service availability. This variation may include longer response times, limited availability of certain features, or temporary use of other models/functionality to accomplish your task. If the service isn’t available, Copilot notifies you. It won’t interrupt any task in progress. For more details, see How Copilot Chat works with and without a Microsoft 365 Copilot license. #### More ways to work with Copilot Chat

  • How Copilot Chat works with and without a Microsoft 365 Copilot license

  • Ask questions and get answers using Microsoft 365 Copilot Chat

  • Microsoft 365 Copilot pricing

  • Convenient interaction: Speak directly with Copilot for a natural, interactive experience.

  • Multitasking: Ask questions and receive information through voice chat while working on other tasks, making your workflow more dynamic and accessible.

Tip

Note

The first time you access any of Copilot voice features, you may be prompted to grant Copilot access to your microphone. You can manage this setting in your browser or device settings. Minimize background noise for accuracy.

  1. Go to Microsoft 365 Copilot or open the Microsoft 365 Copilot app on your desktop, then sign in with your work or school account.

  2. Select Start a new voice chat in the prompt box to start a voice conversation.

    1. You can also start a new voice chat by saying “Hey Copilot” in the Microsoft 365 Copilot app on Windows desktop. Learn more about “Hey Copilot” at How “Hey Copilot” voice chat wake word works in Microsoft 365 Copilot.
    2. If you have the Microsoft 365 Copilot app installed on your Windows desktop, you can also start a voice chat on quick view by pressing Win+C or the Copilot key keyboard shortcuts.

  3. Speak with Copilot, interrupt, redirect, or ask follow-up questions at any time.

  4. To mute or end the conversation, select Mute/pause or End voice chat as needed. At that point, Copilot stops listening.

    1. Always review transcribed or generated content for accuracy as AI may occasionally misinterpret speech or produce errors.

Note

Voice conversations may be time limited and will disconnect automatically after a set period. #### More ways to work with Copilot Chat

Microsoft 365 Copilot: General

Access Microsoft 365 Copilot on Windows

If the Microsoft 365 Copilot app isn’t already on your Windows PC, download the Microsoft 365 Copilot app from the Microsoft Store.

Once installed, open the Copilot app and sign in using your Microsoft 365 work or school account.

You have a several ways to quickly access the Copilot app on your Windows desktop without having to go to your taskbar or Start menu. #### Windows Shortcut Key

In Windows, you can launch the Copilot app at any time in one of the following ways:

  • Press the Copilot key on your keyboard.
  • Using the Windows key + C keyboard shortcut.

The Windows shortcut key can either launch the full application or just a smaller ‘quick view.’ You can configure how these shortcut keys behave by going to Account > Settings > Copilot Keyboard Shortcuts. #### Wake word (“Hey, Copilot”)

You can invoke Copilot by saying aloud the phrase “Hey, Copilot”. This opt-in, hands-free experience helps you stay in your flow when you need answers to a question or just need to bounce an idea off Copilot.

Learn more at How “Hey Copilot” wake word works in Microsoft 365 Copilot. #### Windows pen shortcut button

On supported Windows devices, pens with a configurable shortcut button can be used to invoke the Microsoft 365 Copilot app, but first you’ll need to set up the shortcut button to complete the same action as your Copilot key.

  1. On your Windows PC, go to Settings > Bluetooth & Devices > Pen & Windows Ink.
  2. In the list of your pen’s settings, find the setting for Choose what your shortcut button does and expand the setting’s options menu.
  3. Select the option for Same as Copilot key.

Now, when you press the shortcut key on your pen, the Microsoft 365 Copilot app will open. #### More ways to work with Microsoft 365 Copilot

  4. Choose the widget display you prefer and select Add Widget. #### Use the Microsoft 365 Copilot home screen widget

  1. Touch and hold the lock screen.
  2. Select Customize, and then select Add Widgets.
  3. Scroll through the options and select the desired Copilot widgets you want to add. There are options to open the app, start a voice conversation, start a dictation, and open camera.

Create a video with the Microsoft 365 Copilot app

Last updated: February 2026

[Image: The Create module in the Microsoft 365 Copilot app showing the Create a Video flow.]

With the Microsoft 365 Copilot app, you can have the AI create a short video for you on almost any topic you like. Once the video is created you can edit and refine it to be just what you want.

Note

For users without a Microsoft 365 Copilot license, quality and performance may vary depending on the service availability and may vary throughout the day. If the service isn’t available, Copilot notifies you. It won’t interrupt any task in progress. For more details, see How Copilot Chat works with and without a Microsoft 365 Copilot license.

Note

Clipchamp must be enabled for your organization in order to see and use video creation in Copilot Create. #### How to create a video with the Microsoft 365 Copilot app

Prerequisites

  1. Make sure Clipchamp is enabled for your organization. Only users in organizations where Clipchamp is enabled by their admin can see the Create a video option in Copilot Create. #### Create a video

  2. In the Microsoft 365 Copilot app, select Create from the navigation on the left side of the screen.

  3. Select Create a video.

  4. Give it a description of the video you’d like. Be as specific as possible for the best results. Optionally, you can add a file to help Copilot understand what your video should include. If your organization uses brand kits, you can apply a brand kit to your video.

    [Image: Icon depicting a document with sparkles] Create a video on how to spot a phishing message. Be sure to include information about carefully examining the sender’s address and a reminder never to click on unexpected links or attachments.

    Tip

    If you’d like Copilot to create a video from an existing PowerPoint presentation select Add a PowerPoint file instead of typing a description. Note that the video generated do not inherit the sensitivity label of the sourced file.

  5. Select Create. It usually takes less than a minute to create your video. #### How to edit your video

The video you get may need a bit of refinement. If the changes are relatively minor, like changing the narration voice from masculine to feminine, or choosing a different kind of background music you can select those changes from the Edit with AI panel.

After you select the changes, select Create new draft to create a new version of the video with your changes in it. Don’t worry if you decide the changes aren’t better, you can always go back to any prior version of your video.

If your video needs bigger changes Clipchamp, our online video editor, is ready to help. Select Edit with Clipchamp from the top of the screen.

How to edit a video in Clipchamp #### How to save your video

If you’re happy with your video and ready to save it, select Export and share.

Diagnose issues in a Copilot-generated proposal

[Image: Three-step learning pathway for improving a Copilot-generated proposal. A horizontal progress line highlights step 1, Diagnose, with the Diagnose card visually emphasized while Iterate and Evaluate appear dimmed. The scenario question at the top asks, “How do I improve a Copilot-generated proposal?”]

Microsoft 365 Copilot can generate a polished first draft, but polished doesn’t mean complete. Diagnosing helps you identify missing, unclear, unsupported, or outdated information before revising.

In this scenario, you’ll review a Copilot-generated proposal and identify key gaps before moving on to improving it by iteration. #### Scenario: Review a Copilot-generated proposal draft before changing it

You’re preparing a proposal for executive stakeholders about automating weekly status reporting across several teams. Today, managers collect updates manually, reporting formats vary, and leaders lack a consistent view of risks, delays, and ownership.

The proposal recommends a small pilot before broader rollout. You use Microsoft 365 Copilot to draft the proposal from planning notes, sample reports, team feedback, rollout plans, and time-savings estimates.

At first glance, the proposal appears complete. But polished language can hide important gaps. Some claims are overstated, risks and ownership are unclear, and parts of the timeline might be outdated. Before revising, diagnose where the draft falls short and focus on what matters most. #### How do I apply diagnosis criteria to the proposal draft?

As you review the weekly status reporting proposal, you can use the following criteria to identify what is missing, unclear, or difficult for an executive to act on.

[Image: Visual organizer for diagnosing issues in a Copilot-generated draft. The infographic uses five review checkpoints—Decisions, Risks, Context, Specificity, and Freshness—to uncover missing information and improve proposal quality.]

  • Decisions: Are decisions clearly separated from discussions?
  • Risks: Are blockers, tradeoffs, or uncertainties visible?
  • Context: Would someone outside the discussion understand what matters and why?
  • Specificity: Are statements concrete enough to act on?
  • Freshness: Does the draft reflect the latest plan?

Then apply each criterion to pinpoint where the draft falls short. #### Decisions are unclear

The proposal says leaders “reviewed several rollout options,” but it doesn’t clearly state what decision executive stakeholders need to make now—approve a small pilot, assign staffing, request revisions, or delay the work. The draft mixes discussion with decision-making.

Ask Copilot:

“What decision does this proposal ask executive stakeholders to make, and what still sounds unresolved, assumed, or left open?” #### Risks are underexplained

The proposal presents automation as a straightforward improvement, but your notes show dependencies around staffing, data quality, and adoption readiness. Those risks affect confidence, timing, and rollout scope.

Ask Copilot:

“What risks, dependencies, or uncertainties related to the weekly status reporting pilot are implied but not clearly stated in this draft?” #### Context is missing

The recommendation builds on “existing reporting improvements,” but it never explains the current pain points clearly: teams still gather updates manually, formats differ, and leaders lack a consistent view of risks and delays. Without context, the recommendation feels less urgent and less grounded.

Ask Copilot:

“What background, ownership, or timeline details are missing for an executive reader to understand why this proposal matters now?” #### Language lacks specificity

Phrases like “improve visibility” or “save time” sound useful, but they don’t explain who benefits, what changes, or what phase one includes.

Ask Copilot:

“Which parts of this proposal are too broad or generalized to support action by executive stakeholders?” #### Information might not be fresh

The draft uses a timeline and rollout description that don’t fully match the latest planning notes. The wording sounds reasonable, but might carry forward older assumptions about scope, owners, or launch timing.

Ask Copilot:

“What in this proposal might be out of date, based on earlier versions of the rollout plan or source notes?” #### What does diagnosis look like in practice?

Example: Diagnose a weak proposal paragraph

Copilot draft:

“This initiative will improve cross-team visibility and create meaningful efficiency gains through automation.”

At first glance, this sounds polished—but diagnosis shows why it is not yet strong enough for executive review.

What diagnosis reveals:

  • Area: Decisions; What to look for: Clear outcomes or approved direction.; What’s missing in this draft: The paragraph doesn’t state what leaders are being asked to approve, such as next steps, funding, or a timeline.; Why it matters: Without a clear decision, stakeholders can’t move forward or align on next steps.
  • Area: Risks; What to look for: Blockers, dependencies, or tradeoffs.; What’s missing in this draft: Dependencies such as staffing capacity, adoption readiness, and data consistency are not mentioned.; Why it matters: Missing risks can create false confidence and lead to delays or issues during execution.
  • Area: Context; What to look for: Who, what, when, and why the work matters.; What’s missing in this draft: The draft does not explain the current problem, such as challenges with manual reporting.; Why it matters: Without context, leaders might not understand the urgency or value of the recommendation.
  • Area: Specificity; What to look for: Concrete, actionable detail.; What’s missing in this draft: Phrases like “meaningful efficiency gains” are vague and not tied to measurable outcomes.; Why it matters: Vague language makes it difficult to assess impact or act on the recommendation.
  • Area: Freshness; What to look for: Whether the information reflects the latest state.; What’s missing in this draft: It’s unclear whether the claims align with the most recent scope or time-savings estimates.; Why it matters: Outdated or unverified information can reduce credibility and lead to poor decisions.

Diagnosis makes the gap visible: the draft sounds strong, but it still doesn’t tell leaders enough to make a confident decision. #### Quick readiness check

Before moving on, confirm that you can answer these questions:

✔️ Decisions: What decision is unclear or unstated?

✔️ Risks: What risks, blockers, or dependencies are missing?

✔️ Context: What context about the current reporting problem is assumed rather than explained?

✔️ Specificity: What wording is too vague to support action?

✔️ Freshness: What details might need a freshness check against the latest rollout plan?

If you can clearly name the gaps, the diagnosis is complete. If not, stay in this step before moving on to iteration. Download the Diagnose what’s missing in Copilot output quick reference guide, a practical resource you can revisit as you work. #### Why diagnosing matters

A polished draft can create false confidence. Copilot might produce language that sounds complete while omitting the decisions, risks, context, or current details stakeholders need. Diagnosis helps you slow down, identify what matters most, and revise with a clearer purpose.

Tip

Download the Diagnose what’s missing in Copilot output quick reference guide, a practical resource you can revisit as you work.

Next step: Improve the proposal through iteration #### More ways to build this skill with Copilot

  • Diagnose what’s missing in Copilot output
  • Iterate on Copilot output to get better results
  • Improve a Copilot-generated proposal through iteration

Diagnose what’s missing in Copilot output

[Image: Output diagnosis context card focused on spotting gaps in a Copilot-generated proposal draft. Goal: Spot proposal gaps. Microsoft 365 Copilot license required. App: Microsoft 365 Copilot.]

Microsoft 365 Copilot can help you quickly produce many kinds of output, whether you’re reviewing a summary, an email, an analysis, a plan, a meeting recap, or a policy update. That speed is useful, but a polished response isn’t always a complete one. Before you act on Copilot output, take a moment to inspect and diagnose what might be missing, unclear, weak, over-generalized, or out of date. #### What does it mean to diagnose Copilot output?

Diagnosing means looking at one response and asking: What’s wrong or missing? Before you fix anything, you want to identify the gaps that matter, so you can decide what to question, clarify, or refine before moving forward.

A useful diagnosis is specific. Instead of saying “this feels off,” name the issue: the decisions are unclear, the risks are missing, the context is incomplete, the wording is too vague, or the information might be outdated. That makes the next step easier and helps you avoid acting on output that sounds complete but isn’t. #### How do I diagnose what’s missing in Copilot output?

Use this framework to quickly inspect a single piece of Copilot output and pinpoint what might be missing, unclear, weak, or outdated before you act on it or share it. The framework includes Decisions, Risks, Context, Specificity, and Freshness.

[Image: Visual organizer for Diagnose what’s missing in Copilot output. A five-part framework helps identify gaps in AI-generated content by checking Decisions, Risks, Context, Specificity, and Freshness before revising the output.] #### Decisions

Are decisions clearly stated, or just discussed?

  • What to look for: Check whether the output clearly separates what has been decided from what is still open. A useful response should not blur discussion with outcome.; Signal of weakness: “The team discussed timeline options.”; Ask Copilot: “What decisions are clearly stated in this output?” “What appears unresolved, assumed, or left open?” “Call out any decisions that might be missing or unclear.”

Risks

What risks, blockers, or uncertainties might be missing?

  • What to look for: Look for open risks, blockers, tradeoffs, or uncertainty. If the response sounds too smooth or final, it might be hiding important limits.; Signal of weakness: “Everything is on track and ready to move forward.”; Ask Copilot: “What risks, blockers, or uncertainties are implied but not stated?” “Where might this output sound overconfident or incomplete?” “What tradeoffs should be visible here?”

Context

Does the output include enough context to stand on its own?

  • What to look for: Check whether the output includes the context someone would need to understand it without the original conversation or source material—such as stakeholders, owners, timelines, background, or dependencies.; Signal of weakness: “Next steps were identified, but owners and timelines were not specified.”; Ask Copilot: “Which stakeholders, timelines, or dependencies are missing?” “What background would a new team member need to understand this?” “What context is assumed rather than explained?”

Specificity

Is the output specific enough to act on?

  • What to look for: Watch for vague, generic, or low-signal wording. Good output is specific enough to support understanding or action.; Signal of weakness: “Several updates were shared.” “Key topics were discussed.” “Next steps were outlined.”; Ask Copilot: “What details in this output are too broad or generalized?” “Which parts lack enough detail to act on?” “What important information might have been flattened or omitted?”

Freshness

Does the output reflect the most up-to-date state of the work?

  • What to look for: Check whether the output reflects the latest decisions, updates, and dependencies. Copilot might summarize accurately but still miss recent changes that affect the outcome.; Signal of weakness: “The summary reflects the original plan, but recent changes are not mentioned.”; Ask Copilot: “What in this output might be outdated, assumed, or no longer current?” “What recent updates should be included?” “Where should I confirm the latest status before using this?”

Example: Diagnosing a weak response

Start by looking at the Copilot output as-is, then name what’s missing or unclear using the five diagnosis areas. This is the point of diagnosing: not rewriting the output yet, but identifying why it is not ready to use as-is. #### Copilot output

“The team discussed project timing and cross-team alignment. Next steps were outlined.” #### What diagnosis reveals

  • Diagnosis area: Decisions; What’s missing or unclear: No clear outcome is stated. It’s unclear whether a date was agreed or only discussed.
  • Diagnosis area: Risks; What’s missing or unclear: No blockers, tradeoffs, or uncertainty are visible.
  • Diagnosis area: Context; What’s missing or unclear: No owners, teams, or dependencies are named.
  • Diagnosis area: Specificity; What’s missing or unclear: “Cross-team alignment” and “next steps” are too vague to act on.
  • Diagnosis area: Freshness; What’s missing or unclear: It’s unclear whether this reflects the latest project state.

Quick readiness checklist

Before relying on Copilot output, confirm:

✔️ Decisions: Outcomes are clear, not just discussion.

✔️ Risks: Uncertainty, blockers, or tradeoffs are visible.

✔️ Context: Owners, timelines, dependencies, and background are included.

✔️ Specificity: Details are concrete, not filler language.

✔️ Freshness: The latest known situation is reflected.

If any of these are unclear, follow up before proceeding. Download the Diagnose what’s missing in Copilot output quick reference guide, a practical resource you can revisit as you work. #### Why diagnosing output matters

Copilot accelerates thinking—but speed can hide gaps. When output looks fluent, it’s easy to assume it’s complete and move on. Diagnosing slows you down just enough to catch what could mislead decisions, create rework, or erode trust if shared too soon.

By diagnosing before you act, you:

  • Prevent false confidence. Clear language can mask missing decisions, risks, or assumptions.
  • Protect downstream work. Catching gaps early avoids costly revisions, misalignment, or follow-up meetings.
  • Improve collaboration. Naming what’s missing helps stakeholders focus on the right questions, not just the output.
  • Use Copilot more effectively. Diagnosis turns vague dissatisfaction into specific signals you can refine next.

In short, diagnosing is how you stay in control of judgment. Copilot helps you move faster—but diagnosing ensures you move in the right direction.

Tip

Download the Diagnose what’s missing in Copilot output quick reference guide, a practical resource you can revisit as you work.
