Quick tips for better prompts

Personalize what Microsoft 365 Copilot remembers

Microsoft 365 Copilot offers you tailored experiences by remembering key details and preferences from your conversations with Microsoft 365 Copilot Chat using Copilot Memory. Over time, it can recall useful information like your communication style, favorite topics, work goals, and recurring tasks to make conversations straightforward and more relevant. #### Personalize what Copilot Chat remembers

Copilot personalizes your chat experience and its responses by:

  • Saving memories to Copilot Memory
  • Making inferences from your chat history
  • Using your Copilot custom instructions
  • Forgetting what was discussed in a temporary chat #### Save memories using Copilot Chat

When Copilot Memory is turned on, Copilot automatically saves key information to its memory from your Copilot Chat conversations.

Learn how to manage saved memories in Copilot Chat at Manage Copilot Memory in Microsoft 365 Copilot. #### Revisit your chat history

Copilot keeps a history of your previous conversations with Copilot Chat. Copilot can make inferences about what matters to you using previous conversations to respond to subsequent conversations.

Learn how to revisit your chat history at Revisit your Microsoft 365 Copilot Chat history. #### Add custom instructions to Copilot settings

To further personalize how Copilot responds to you, you can create custom instructions that tell Copilot important information to remember, like your communication preferences.

Learn how to manage custom instructions for Copilot at Customize how Microsoft 365 Copilot responds to you. #### Use temporary chat to limit what Copilot Chat remembers

If you want to chat without using or updating memory, you can Start a temporary chat. These conversations won’t access or store any personalized information. Also, the conversation won’t appear in your chat history and can’t be referenced or accessed later by you or Copilot.

However, temporary chat data may still be retained according to your organization’s data retention policy and could be accessible by your IT administrator during the data retention period.

To start a temporary chat, complete the following steps:

  1. Go to Copilot Chat and sign in with your Microsoft 365 account.
  2. At the top-right of the Copilot Chat page, select [Image: Icon of text message bubble for the temporary chat button in Copilot Chat] Start a new temporary chat button.
  3. Chat with Copilot like you normally would. #### How does Microsoft handle Copilot Memory data?

Copilot Memory is handled with the same care as your other work data in Microsoft 365 Copilot.

To learn more about Copilot Chat data handling standards, see Frequently asked questions about Microsoft 365 Copilot Chat. #### How do I fully delete something Copilot knows about me?

If Copilot Memory is turned on and you want to completely remove something Copilot has remembered, you need to take two steps:

  1. Delete the saved memory from your Chat settings. Learn how to delete saved memories at Delete information from Copilot Memory.

    1. This ensures Copilot no longer retains the specific detail you asked it to remember.

  2. Memories from your chat history can be deleted by switching off the Chat history personalization setting, which deletes any inferences Copilot has made from previous Copilot Chat conversations, or by contacting your IT admin. Learn how to delete chat history data at How do I turn off Copilot personalization based on chat history?

    1. If the Chat history personalization setting is turned back on within 30 days after the deletion, Copilot adds back the deleted memories from your chat history. #### More ways to work with Copilot Chat
  • Get started with Microsoft 365 Copilot Chat
  • Ask questions and get answers using Microsoft 365 Copilot Chat
  • Catch up on things quickly using Microsoft 365 Copilot Chat
  • Create content using Microsoft 365 Copilot Chat
  • Get started with voice features in Microsoft 365 Copilot

Refer to specific files and more in Microsoft 365 Copilot

Refer to specific files, people, Teams conversations, and more in Copilot Chat, which enhances grounding and collaboration by making information, people, and conversations more accessible.

When you initiate the references menu, relevant content is suggested based on scanning of your files, emails, conversations, and other Microsoft 365 apps and services. It also allows you to insert multiple items including links to Microsoft 365 files, images, people, meetings, chats, and emails. Copilot refers to the content you include as part of its response. #### Users with a Microsoft 365 Copilot license (Microsoft 365 Copilot users)

Work IQ turned on

To add links to Microsoft 365 files, reference people, and include calendar events and emails, follow these steps:

  1. Go to Copilot Chat and sign in with your Microsoft 365 account.

  2. At the top right of the chat page, make sure the Work IQ button is turned on.

  3. To reference a specific piece of information, type / and begin typing the people, file, meeting, or email you wish to link.

    [Image: Screenshot of the open references menu.]

  4. People, Microsoft 365 files, meetings, and emails are accessible through tabs. When you select a tab, the menu options update to provide suggestions based on their relevance to you.

  5. Select from the people, files, meetings, and emails listed.

Note

If you write a prompt and don’t reference a specific file, person, or meeting, Copilot tries to determine the best source of data for its response, including your work content. #### Work IQ turned off

Note

When Work IQ is turned off, Copilot can still reference publicly available information from the web, if your IT administrator has allowed it. Learn more about web search in Copilot at How web search works in Microsoft 365 Copilot Chat and agents.

Only files are available to reference when you’ve turned off Work IQ in chat. To add links to files, follow these steps:

  1. Go to Copilot Chat and sign in with your Microsoft 365 account.
  2. At the top right of the chat page, turn off the Work IQ button.
  3. To reference a specific file, type / and begin typing the file name you wish to link.
  4. From the Files tab, the menu provides suggestions based on their relevance to you.
  5. Select from the files listed. #### Users without a Microsoft 365 Copilot license (Copilot Chat users)

Note

If you don’t have a Microsoft 365 Copilot license, Copilot can still reference publicly available information from the web, if your IT administrator has allowed it. Learn more about web search in Copilot at How web search works in Microsoft 365 Copilot Chat and agents.

Only files are available to reference for users without a Microsoft 365 Copilot license. To add links to files, follow these steps:

  1. Go to Copilot Chat and sign in with your Microsoft 365 account.
  2. To reference a specific file, type / and begin typing the file you wish to link.
  3. From the Files tab, the menu provides suggestions based on their relevance to you.
  4. Select from the files listed. #### Reference specific types of content in Copilot Chat

Referencing people

Copilot suggests people based on how well their names or email addresses match what you’re typing. The system considers how often and recently you’ve communicated with them via Teams, emails, meetings, or shared files, updating this ranking several times a day. #### Referencing meetings

Copilot suggests meetings based on your RSVPs and recent or upcoming events, matching either the meeting title or the organizer’s name. Copilot can also suggest meeting series and specific meetings in a series. #### Referencing Teams content

Copilot suggests your relevant Teams group and meeting chats based on recent activity and number of participants under the Chats tab. Copilot also displays your recently used Teams channels under the Channels tab. All channel types are supported, including private, shared, and general channels. #### Referencing emails

Copilot displays relevant emails based on your interactions, such as mentions, flags, and focused emails, as well as matching subjects, senders, keywords, attachments, or recipients. Copilot can also reference emails in shared and delegated mailboxes. #### Referencing files and images

Copilot suggests relevant files and images based on recent activity and interactions, including those recently uploaded, opened, modified, or shared on OneDrive for Business. Files are available a couple of minutes after any of these actions. Files are also recommended based on file names, types, or authors.

  • Local files and images: To reference a local file, in the Copilot Chat compose box, select [Image: Add plus sign icon] Add and manage sources and then [Image: 358ec935-76b6-477f-8c6d-5813b150ebec] Upload images and files from the menu. You can then select multiple images or files from your local device to reference. Once the file is selected, a copy is saved to OneDrive.

  • Cloud files: To reference a file available on a cloud server, select [Image: OneDrive icon] Attach cloud files. You can attach either a file or an entire folder to your prompt.

    Note

    In addition to typing /, you can also reference files using copy and paste or drag and drop.

    You can only reference up to ten files or pages within a SharePoint site.

To learn more about supported files, see the Microsoft 365 Copilot (Work) section of File formats supported by Copilot. #### Referencing SharePoint sites

Copilot displays SharePoint sites, pages, and lists that you’ve recently interacted with or were shared with you. Select a site to scope your prompt to its contents.

Note

Up to ten files or pages can be referenced within the selected SharePoint site.

Known limitations to referencing SharePoint lists

  • Copilot supports referencing up to 20,000 list items at a time.
  • Currently, referencing attachment columns or lookup columns in SharePoint lists isn’t supported. #### Referencing Connectors

You can now ground your Copilot prompts on third-party data using your organization’s Graph Connector content, accessible from the references menu in Copilot Chat. For any Connectors you have enabled in Copilot, you can access them from the All and Other tabs, in the menu that appears after typing /.

Examples of supported Connectors include Confluence, Jira, and ServiceNow. #### More ways to work with Copilot Chat

  • Get started with Microsoft 365 Copilot Chat
  • Frequently asked questions about Microsoft 365 Copilot Chat
  • Data, Privacy, and Security for Microsoft 365 Copilot

Schedule a meeting to collaborate on a file with the Microsoft 365 Copilot app

Collaborating on a file and want to meet with a colleague to discuss it? You can schedule that meeting right from the Microsoft 365 Copilot app.

[Image: A context menu in the Microsoft 365 Copilot app showing that you can add a file to either Calendar or To Do.] #### How to schedule a meeting about a file

  1. In the Microsoft 365 Copilot app, select Search from the navigation pane on the left.

  2. Scroll down into the Quick access section to find the file you want to meet about.

  3. Hover over the filename and select the More actions [Image: more actions button-duplicate] button that appears to the right of the file name.

  4. Select Add to and then Calendar.

    Tip

    Want to add it to your task list instead? Select To Do instead of Calendar.

  5. An Outlook calendar item will open, with a link to the file in the notes section. Add any other attendees you want, select a date and time, then send it. #### Other ways to schedule a meeting

  • Schedule a meeting using Copilot

Share a Microsoft 365 Copilot Page

When you’re ready to collaborate in Microsoft 365 Copilot Pages, it’s easy to share your page with others. They’ll have access to the page and its content, allowing them to contribute without accessing your Copilot chat session.

Note

  • This article applies to Copilot Pages in the Microsoft 365 Copilot app. If you want to learn about Copilot Pages in the Microsoft Copilot app, see Using Copilot Pages with Microsoft Copilot.
  • If you have a work or school (Entra ID) account, Copilot Pages is available to those who have SharePoint or OneDrive storage, including ones without a Microsoft 365 Copilot license.
  • If you have a personal Microsoft account, Copilot Pages is available if you’re a Microsoft 365 Personal, Family, or Premium subscriber. Learn more about Microsoft 365 Copilot licensing and Microsoft 365 Copilot plans. #### How to share your page
  1. Select Share [Image: Share icon in Teams for iOS] in the upper right corner of your page.
  2. Select one of the following:

  • Choose Page link to copy and paste the link in Teams, Outlook, or other Microsoft 365 apps. Colleagues will only have access to this singular page, which will open in Loop.

    [Image: Copilot Pages option to share a page through a page link.]

  • Choose Copy component to copy and paste the page, which appears as a component, in Microsoft Teams, Outlook, OneNote, or other Microsoft 365 apps. Changes made to the component within Microsoft 365 apps will update the page in real time.

    [Image: Copilot Pages share a page option of copy component.] #### More ways to work with Copilot Pages

Supported languages for Microsoft 365 Copilot

Last updated: February 2026

This article lists the languages that are currently supported in Microsoft 365 Copilot. For any supported languages:

  • You can successfully input text as prompts in all modalities in all features for Microsoft 365 Copilot.
  • Microsoft 365 Copilot features can function using grounding data.
  • Microsoft 365 Copilot features can produce output in all modalities.
  • Microsoft 365 Copilot features are fully localized.
  • Feature teams will respond to and fix customer issues feedback, and bugs logged.
  • Output quality (functional and linguistic) in the language has been tested broadly across all Microsoft 365 Copilot features and meets Microsoft’s quality requirements for an enterprise release.

If you’re using Microsoft 365 Copilot and you’re getting an error that indicates a language isn’t currently supported, it means that Microsoft 365 Copilot cannot process information in that language at this time. You can get an unsupported-language message even if the user interface (UI) is translated into your language.

This article describes what to do if you run into an unsupported-language issue and lists the languages that are currently supported.

Note

Information on this page applies to Microsoft 365 Copilot. If you’re looking for supported languages in Microsoft Copilot for personal use, please see Supported languages for Microsoft Copilot. #### What languages are currently supported in Microsoft 365 Copilot prompts and responses?

In general, Copilot supports the following languages for prompts:

  • Albanian

  • Arabic

  • Bulgarian

  • Chinese (Simplified)

  • Chinese (Traditional)

  • Croatian

  • Catalan

  • Czech

  • Danish

  • Dutch

  • English (United Kingdom)

  • English (United States)

  • Estonian

  • Filipino

  • Finnish

  • French (France)

  • French (Canada)

  • German

  • Greek

  • Hebrew

  • Hungarian

  • Icelandic

  • Indonesian

  • Italian

  • Japanese

  • Korean

  • Latvian

  • Lithuanian

  • Malay

  • Maltese

  • Norwegian (Bokmål)

  • Polish

  • Portuguese (Brazil)

  • Portuguese (Portugal)

  • Romanian

  • Russian

  • Serbian (Cyrillic)

  • Serbian (Latin script)

  • Slovak

  • Slovenian

  • Spanish

  • Spanish (Mexico)

  • Swedish

  • Thai

  • Turkish

  • Ukrainian

  • Vietnamese

  • Welsh #### What causes an unsupported-language error?

Currently, Microsoft 365 Copilot supports fewer languages than what’s available for the UI. So although you can choose from lots of languages for the UI in Teams and Microsoft 365 Apps, Copilot works with a fewer number of languages.

If you type a prompt in Copilot, and the language you’re using or requesting isn’t currently supported, you’ll get an error message. #### What do I do if I get a language error in Microsoft 365 Copilot?

Copilot provides responses to prompts that are written in supported languages.

If you type a prompt that requests content in another language that isn’t currently supported, Copilot displays a message telling you to change your prompt and try again. If you run into this issue, use one of the supported languages for your prompt and try again. #### Will more languages be added to Microsoft 365 Copilot?

Microsoft is planning to add more languages to Copilot. This article will be updated as additional languages are supported. #### Learn more

Change the language Office uses in its menus and proofing tools

Where can I get Microsoft Copilot?

Understand changes to Microsoft 365 Copilot mobile app

Last updated: February 2026

The Microsoft 365 Copilot mobile app is rolling out changes for both Microsoft 365 subscribers and non-subscribers. These updates affect how you access and edit your files and how you create new Word, Excel, and PowerPoint files on mobile.

As the app moves to an AI-first experience, some capabilities such as browsing your full list of files and editing Microsoft Word, Excel, and PowerPoint files inside the Microsoft 365 Copilot mobile app will be discontinued. Instead, you’ll be redirected to open or install dedicated Word, Excel, PowerPoint, and OneDrive mobile apps for those tasks. How you create new files in the Microsoft 365 mobile app is also changing for Microsoft 365 subscribers and non-subscribers.

The file browsing, creation, and editing functionality that exists in the mobile app today will still be available for both non-subscribers and subscribers, just through different access points.

See the following sections for how the changes will affect non-subscribers and those with an active Microsoft 365 subscription. #### Changes for Microsoft 365 non-subscribers

When you try to open a file in the Microsoft 365 Copilot mobile app, you’ll be redirected to the appropriate app (Word, Excel, or PowerPoint) where you can access and edit the file. Sign in to that app with the Microsoft account that has access to the file.

How to access files, create and edit Word, Excel, PowerPoint files

To create a new Word, Excel, or PowerPoint file, you’ll need to use the dedicated Word, Excel, and PowerPoint mobile apps.

You can browse your most recent files in the Search tab, however, to browse and manage your full list of files, you’ll be redirected to the OneDrive mobile app.

If you don’t have Word, Excel, PowerPoint, or OneDrive currently installed, you can find them in your app store, available for both iOS and Android. #### Changes for Microsoft 365 subscribers

If you have a Microsoft 365 subscription, when opening the mobile app, you’ll land on the Chat home screen where you can start a voice or text chat to ask Copilot questions, summarize content, brainstorm new ideas, and create documents and visual content. This includes personal subscriptions and eligible work or school subscriptions where Copilot is available to your organization.

How to access, create, and edit Microsoft Word, Excel and PowerPoint files

You can browse your most recent files or search for files in the Search tab of the Microsoft 365 Copilot app. To browse and manage your full list of files, you’ll be redirected to the OneDrive mobile app.

When opening a Word, Excel, or PowerPoint file in the Microsoft 365 Copilot mobile app, you can preview the file and use Copilot Chat to ask questions about the files directly in the previewer. To edit that document, you will be redirected to the appropriate Word, Excel, or PowerPoint mobile app.

To create a Word, Excel, or PowerPoint file, use the Chat tab to prompt Copilot to create a Word, Excel, or PowerPoint file, describing the file you want to create. Copilot will create a download link for the file. Select the link and you will be redirected to the appropriate mobile app to open and edit the file.

If you don’t have Word, Excel, PowerPoint, or OneDrive currently installed, you can find them in your app store, available for both iOS and Android.

Validate a Copilot-generated decision brief before you share it

[Image: Three-step learning pathway for creating a trusted executive decision brief. A horizontal progress line highlights step 3, Validate, with the Validate card visually emphasized while Decide and Design appear inactive. The scenario question at the top asks, “How do I shape an executive decision brief I can trust?”]

A decision brief can read well and still be risky to share. A date might be wrong, a caveat might be missing, or a recommendation might go further than the evidence supports. That is why validation matters.

This scenario shows how to validate a Copilot-generated decision brief to bridge the gap between having a draft and trusting it enough to share with stakeholders. #### Scenario: Review your draft decision brief before you share it

You used Copilot to create a first draft of a decision brief for senior leaders about whether to continue, change, or stop a pilot after mixed results. The draft pulls together pilot findings, feedback themes, meeting notes, adoption data, early cost estimates, recommendations, major risks, and next steps.

It might feel close to ready, but the stakes are high. This decision brief could influence whether leadership invests more, expands the pilot, changes the approach, or ends the effort. If it overstates adoption, omits important caveats, treats estimates as facts, or recommends a direction the evidence doesn’t support, it could lead to poor decisions.

Before you share it, you slow down and review what the draft is claiming. You check whether the recommendation matches the source material, whether key statements are supported, caveats are included, and the conclusion is strong enough to stand behind.

Copilot can help you identify gaps and surface questions, but you remain responsible for deciding whether the decision brief is accurate, complete, and ready to share. #### What checks should I use to validate the decision brief?

Before you share the decision brief, validate it using these four checks: Source, Verified, Context, and Resilient.

[Image: Visual organizer for validating a Copilot-generated decision brief before acting. The framework verifies Source, Evidence, Context, and Resilience to ensure the information can be trusted.] #### Source

Does the decision brief accurately reflect the pilot evidence it came from?

  • Do: Trace key claims, recommendations, timelines, and risks back to source material.; Don’t: Assume statements are accurate without checking the source.; Ask Copilot: “Compare this decision brief with the source material and highlight mismatches or unsupported claims.”
  • Do: Confirm summaries preserve the original meaning and intent.; Don’t: Rewrite or compress content in a way that changes meaning.; Ask Copilot: “Flag missing qualifications or context from the source.”
  • Do: Treat anything untraceable as unconfirmed and request supporting evidence.; Don’t: Leave unsupported or inferred claims in place.; Ask Copilot: “Suggest where supporting evidence is needed.”

Verified

Are the critical details confirmed?

  • Do: Verify dates, numbers, budgets, owners, approvals, and scope boundaries.; Don’t: Reuse details that haven’t been confirmed.; Ask Copilot: “Identify which facts, names, dates, and numbers require verification.”
  • Do: Cross-check high-impact details against reliable sources.; Don’t: Assume earlier drafts or estimates are still valid.; Ask Copilot: “Point to where each detail should be confirmed.”
  • Do: Resolve ambiguous, conflicting, outdated, or placeholder details before sharing.; Don’t: Treat “close enough” details as acceptable.; Ask Copilot: “Flag unclear or conflicting details.”

Context

What is missing that could change the decision?

  • Do: Include assumptions, dependencies, constraints, and unresolved risks.; Don’t: Present conclusions without supporting conditions or feasibility factors.; Ask Copilot: “Identify missing context that could change the recommendation.”
  • Do: Clarify what must be true for timelines or outcomes to hold.; Don’t: Make timelines or outcomes sound final when they depend on conditions.; Ask Copilot: “Highlight conditions or uncertainties that affect the recommendation, such as adoption, staffing, cost, or unresolved risks.”
  • Do: Make implicit context explicit so stakeholders can interpret the decision brief accurately.; Don’t: Assume stakeholders share the same background knowledge.; Ask Copilot: “Flag gaps that could lead to misinterpretation.”

Resilient

Would this hold up if conditions change?

  • Do: Stress-test assumptions against different scenarios, audiences, and interpretations.; Don’t: Assume best-case conditions will hold or that everyone will interpret the decision brief the same way.; Ask Copilot: “Identify scenarios that could break or weaken the decision brief.”
  • Do: Evaluate how adoption shifts, dependency changes, costs, or delays could affect outcomes.; Don’t: Ignore downstream effects of changing assumptions or slipping dependencies.; Ask Copilot: “Surface questions stakeholders might ask.”
  • Do: Clarify uncertainty, conditional outcomes, and areas that might need flexibility.; Don’t: Present conditional outcomes as guaranteed commitments.; Ask Copilot: “Suggest where to add flexibility or clarification.”

Example: How validation changes a risky statement

  • Before validation: The pilot was successful, so we should move forward with broader expansion.; After validation: The pilot shows encouraging results in some teams, but uneven adoption, open staffing questions, and unresolved implementation risks should be addressed before recommending broader expansion.
  • Before validation: The initiative will reduce operational costs by 30% within the first year.; After validation: Based on the pilot results, the initiative could reduce operational costs by an estimated 15–30% within the first year, depending on adoption rates, implementation costs, and the final rollout model.

The after versions are not just more cautious. They are more accurate, more useful, and less likely to mislead. #### How can Copilot support validation without replacing human review?

Copilot can support the validation process. It can act as a:

  • Reviewer that compares the draft with your sources.
  • Challenger that surfaces assumptions.
  • Gap finder that points out missing context.
  • Stress tester that explores edge cases.

That support is useful, but it is not final proof that the draft is correct. For higher-stakes content, validation still depends on human judgment and trusted source material. #### Quick readiness checklist

Before you share a Copilot-generated decision brief, confirm:

✔️ Source: Key claims match the source material.

✔️ Verified: Important facts and details are confirmed.

✔️ Context: Nothing important has been left out.

✔️ Resilient: The decision brief still holds up across questions, exceptions, and audiences.

If any one of those is unclear, validate further before sending it. Download the Validate Copilot output before you act on it quick reference guide, a practical resource you can revisit as you work. #### Why validation matters

Copilot can get you to a useful decision brief draft faster, but validation is what makes that draft responsible to share. It helps you catch weak claims, missing caveats, unsupported conclusions, and overconfident language before they affect decision-making.

Copilot can help draft and review the decision brief. You decide whether it is accurate, complete, and trustworthy enough to leave your hands.

Responsible Copilot use depends on thoughtful delegation, clear boundaries, and careful validation before you share the final decision brief.

Tip

Download the Validate Copilot output before you act on it quick reference guide, a practical resource you can revisit as you work. #### More ways to build this skill

  • Design boundaries for a Copilot-generated decision brief
  • Validate Copilot output before you act on it
  • Get started with Microsoft 365 Copilot - Training

Validate Copilot output before you act on it

[Image: Output validation context card focused on validating a Copilot-generated decision brief before acting on it. Goal: Confirm brief accuracy. Microsoft 365 Copilot license required. App: Microsoft 365 Copilot.]

Microsoft 365 Copilot can help you move faster. It can summarize meetings, draft communications, analyze information, and pull together ideas from multiple sources in seconds. That speed is useful—but speed is not the same as readiness.

A response can sound polished and still be wrong, incomplete, or risky to use. Validation is the step between getting a response and trusting it. #### What is validation—and what isn’t?

Validation means checking Copilot output after it is generated and before you act on it, share it, or rely on it in a decision. It is not the same as polishing: a response can become clearer, shorter, or more professional and still be unsafe to use.

For example:

  • A summary might read better but still leave out the risk that changes the decision.
  • A customer reply might sound polished but include an unsupported claim.
  • An analysis might look convincing but rely on assumptions that were never checked.

If you are asking, “Can I trust this enough to move forward?”, you are in validation mode. #### Why does validation matter when using Copilot?

Using AI doesn’t transfer accountability. If Copilot output influences a recommendation, shapes a decision, or gets shared with other people, you still own the outcome.

Validation matters most when the output will be used to:

  • Support a decision or recommendation.
  • Summarize information from multiple sources.
  • Communicate with customers, partners, or leadership.
  • Identify priorities, risks, or next steps.
  • Guide work that is time-sensitive or hard to reverse later.

In those situations, small errors can create outsized consequences. Often the real issue is a missing caveat, a dropped dependency, or an overconfident conclusion. #### How do I validate Copilot output before using it?

Use four simple checks to validate Copilot output: Source, Verified, Context, and Resilient. These checks help you catch errors, gaps, and risks before you act.

[Image: Visual organizer for validating Copilot output before taking action. Four verification checks—Source, Verified, Context, and Resilient—help confirm reliability and reduce risk.] #### Source

Does the response accurately reflect the source material?

  • What to check: Do the claims match the original files, notes, messages, or data? Can you trace key statements back to a reliable source? Has Copilot blended ideas, overstated certainty, or filled gaps with assumptions?; Why it matters: Prevents unsupported or distorted claims.; Ask Copilot: “Cross-check this response against the source material and highlight any mismatches, unsupported claims, or missing qualifications.”

If a statement can’t be traced back to a source, treat it as unconfirmed until verified. #### Verified

Are the most important facts, names, dates, numbers, and recommendations confirmed?

  • What to check: Which details matter most if they are wrong? What needs independent confirmation? Are you trusting something because it sounds right or because it was checked?; Why it matters: Reduces risk of errors in critical facts.; Ask Copilot: “Which statements in this response require verification, and where should I confirm each one?”

A response can be useful overall but still contain specific details that shouldn’t be used as-is. #### Context

What is missing that could change the outcome or decision?

  • What to check: Are key caveats, assumptions, dependencies, or risks missing? Is there an exception that changes the guidance? Would the recommendation change if a missing detail were included?; Why it matters: Ensures context, caveats, and risks are included.; Ask Copilot: “What important context, caveats, dependencies, or risks might be missing from this response?”

Many AI mistakes are really omissions. The output might sound complete because it is smooth and well organized, but the missing context is what makes it misleading. #### Resilient

Would this response hold up in different scenarios?

  • What to check: What if conditions change? What if a different audience reads this? What exceptions or alternate interpretations could make this misleading?; Why it matters: Tests reliability beyond the simplest case.; Ask Copilot: “What scenarios, exceptions, or alternate interpretations could make this response incorrect or misleading?”

A fragile response might be fine in the easiest version of the situation and still be unsafe to use more widely. #### How do I apply the validation framework in a real scenario?

For this scenario, Copilot gives you this recommendation: “You can recommend a company-wide launch next week. No major blockers remain.”

At first glance, that sounds clear and confident. But before you repeat or act on it, validate it. The following table shows how each check helps you test the response for hidden risk.

  • Validation check: Source; What to check in this scenario: Does the source material actually say that no major blockers remain?; Risk: The recommendation might overstate what was said or imply a decision that was never made.; Action: Check the meeting transcript, notes, or source files before repeating the recommendation.
  • Validation check: Verified; What to check in this scenario: Have legal approval, dates, and readiness details been confirmed?; Risk: Key details might be outdated, incomplete, or incorrect.; Action: Verify important facts before taking action or sharing the recommendation.
  • Validation check: Context; What to check in this scenario: Were any important rollout conditions left out, such as regional exceptions or dependencies?; Risk: The response might sound complete but leave out critical constraints.; Action: Add the missing context before using the recommendation.
  • Validation check: Resilient; What to check in this scenario: Would this recommendation still hold true across all teams, regions, or audiences?; Risk: Guidance that works in one situation might fail in another.; Action: Adjust the recommendation so it fits the full business context.

After checking, you might find that legal review is still pending or that regional differences affect timing. Instead of repeating the recommendation, you confirm those issues first.

That is what validation does: it helps you catch hidden weakness before it turns into a bad decision. #### How can Copilot help me validate its own output?

Copilot can help you validate its output—but it can’t certify its own correctness.

This means you can use Copilot as a:

  • Reviewer, to compare output with sources.
  • Challenger, to surface assumptions.
  • Gap finder, to identify missing context.
  • Stress tester, to explore edge cases. #### Try these prompts

Source check

[Image: Icon depicting a document with sparkles] Compare this response with the referenced source material and highlight any statements that are unsupported, overstated, or inconsistent with the original content.

Verified check

[Image: Icon depicting a document with sparkles] Which facts, names, dates, numbers, or recommendations in this response should be independently verified, and where should I confirm each one?

Context check

[Image: Icon depicting a document with sparkles] What important context, caveats, dependencies, or risks might be missing from this response that could affect how it should be interpreted or used?

Resilient check

[Image: Icon depicting a document with sparkles] “What scenarios, exceptions, or audience differences could make this response misleading, incomplete, or unsafe to use as written?”

Use Copilot’s feedback as a signal—not as the final authority. Always verify against trusted sources when the stakes matter. #### Quick readiness checklist

Before you act on or share Copilot output, confirm:

✔️ Source: The content matches the source material.

✔️ Verified: Key facts have been confirmed.

✔️ Context: Nothing important is missing.

✔️ Resilient: The content holds up across scenarios and audiences. #### Accountability stays with you

Microsoft 365 Copilot can help you process more information, move faster, and generate drafts quickly. But once that output enters real work, human judgment is what makes it trustworthy.

Validation ensures Copilot-generated work is not only useful, but also accurate, reliable, and safe to use in real-world situations.

Tip

Download the Validate Copilot output before you act on it quick reference guide, a practical resource you can revisit as you work. #### More ways to build this skill

  • Evaluate Copilot output for clarity, accuracy, tone, and context
  • Decide when Copilot or an agent is the right tool for your work
  • Get started with Microsoft 365 Copilot — Training

[Embedded video omitted]

Copilot is your AI assistant, ready to help you be more creative and more productive. In the Microsoft 365 Copilot app you can upload or create files, ask questions, collaborate on AI-generated content, set up agents that help you be more productive, and add a wide array of custom agents and apps. #### Why use the Microsoft 365 Copilot app

  • Start your day: Get answers, generate content, access your files

  • Security: Includes enterprise data protection

  • Copilot on the go: Available on the web, desktop, and mobile devices #### Where to find Microsoft 365 Copilot

  • From your browser sign into m365.cloud.microsoft with your work or school account.

  • For Windows devices, download the Microsoft 365 Copilot app from the Microsoft Store.

  • For iOS or Android, see Get the Microsoft 365 Copilot mobile app. #### How to use Microsoft 365 Copilot

Some of them, such as Chat, Library, and Search, are always there. Others, like Pinned allow you to pin agents and apps to the left-side navigation for easier access.

When you open the app, you should see your name and avatar at the bottom left of the app. Get started using Copilot Chat to create content, catch up quickly, and ask questions.

What’s the difference between Microsoft Copilot (free) and Copilot in Microsoft 365

Copilot is an AI assistant designed to help you complete tasks faster and more efficiently. It uses large language models (LLMs) to respond to your questions or prompts. Copilot is integrated into many different Microsoft apps and services including Microsoft 365 subscriptions, and you might need help understanding what works best for your needs and what you might already have access to.

This article describes the differences between the free version of Copilot, and the version of Copilot included in individual and business Microsoft 365 subscriptions.

  • Microsoft Copilot (free) - This version of Copilot is available free of cost and is ideal for general questions and answers, people trying AI for the first time, and web-based tasks. You can access Copilot in your web browser at copilot.microsoft.com, download as an app on your desktop or mobile device, or use directly in Copilot in Edge.
  • Copilot in eligible individual Microsoft 365 plans - Copilot is available in individual subscriptions such as Microsoft 365 Personal, Family, or Premium and subscribers can access Copilot Chat in their web browser at microsoft365.com or download the app to your mobile device. The subscription owner has access to Copilot in the desktop apps which includes deep integration with documents, emails, spreadsheets, calendars and more. Usage limits apply (AI credits and limits for Microsoft 365 Personal, Family, and Premium).
  • Copilot in Microsoft 365 business plans - Access to Copilot in eligible Microsoft 365 business plans depends on your type of license. Users with a business subscription have access to AI chat grounded on the web, pay-as-you-go agents, built-in IT controls, enterprise data protection, and agent management. Depending on how your organization is configured you might also have access to Copilot directly in Microsoft 365 apps like Word, Excel, PowerPoint, OneNote.

Learn more about each of these versions in the sections below. #### In this article

  • Microsoft Copilot (free)
  • Copilot in Microsoft 365 individual plans
  • Copilot in Microsoft 365 business plans #### Microsoft Copilot (free)

Microsoft Copilot is available at no cost at copilot.microsoft.com.

Microsoft Copilot is your AI companion and understands your questions and requests, provides direct answers, assists with writing, and even creates images. Sign in isn’t required, but signing in with your personal account gives you access to chat history, image creation, longer conversations, voice interactions, and other Copilot features.

Access Copilot on Windows, macOS, mobile devices (iOS and Android), and Microsoft Edge header.

Microsoft Copilot (free) allows you to:

  • Chat with AI for help with writing, brainstorming, summarizing, and more.
  • Get real-time answers based on web search.
  • Use Copilot in Edge for webpage summarization and insights.

Learn more at Getting started with Microsoft Copilot.

  • AI-powered Chat that can respond using your work data like files, emails, chats, people, and more.
  • Included access to agents based on your work data and to reasoning agents like Researcher and Analyst
  • Advanced features both in-app and in Copilot Chat in Microsoft 365 apps
  • Meeting summaries and recaps in Microsoft Teams (if available)
  • Premium AI search and creation functionality
  • More security, privacy, and compliance features for IT admins

The Microsoft 365 Copilot license includes priority access for faster response times and more consistent availability.

Microsoft 365 Copilot: Notebooks

Add references to your Microsoft 365 Copilot Notebook

Last updated: June 2026

With Microsoft 365 Copilot Notebooks, you can create and reason with Copilot in a unified space that brings together all your relevant content. As you work with Copilot, receive focused answers tailored to your notebook’s content, and you can add more references as your project evolves. References stay current, so as your data changes, the answers from notebook prompts remain accurate and up to date.

Note

Copilot Notebooks is available to Microsoft 365 Copilot and Copilot Chat licensed users. Accounts must have a SharePoint or OneDrive license (service plan) to create notebooks. Copilot Notebooks is also available for Microsoft 365 Personal, Family, and Premium subscribers. Learn more about Microsoft 365 Copilot licensing and Microsoft 365 Copilot plans.

Note

Some features described in this article are rolling out gradually and may not yet be available in your experience. #### How to add references to your notebook

  1. In the Microsoft 365 Copilot app under Notebooks, select an existing notebook.

    Note

    New to Copilot Notebooks? Learn how to create your first notebook.

  2. Under References in the left pane, select the plus button

    next to References.

  3. Under Add references, Copilot will suggest a list of references you may want to add. You can also add references in a few different ways.

    • Search – In the search bar, enter the name of a specific reference, then select it from the list of suggested references.

      You can also search for and add SharePoint sites, document libraries, or folders as references. When you select one, Copilot can use supported files stored in that location to answer questions, summarize information, or generate new content based on what you have permission to access. Note Copilot only uses content from the sites, folders, or files that you add as references and have permission to access. It doesn’t use other organizational content unless you add those locations to your notebook.

    • Upload – Select the Upload button [Image: upload] and find the file you want to add.

    • Link – Select the Link button [Image: link] and enter the URL you want to add. You can add links to documents stored in your organization’s OneDrive or SharePoint as references in a notebook. Links to content outside your organization can’t be added.

    • OneDrive – Select the OneDrive icon, choose an individual file or entire folder from your OneDrive to add as a reference, and then select Add.

    • Drag and drop – Drop your file into the section, Drag your files and other content here.

  4. Select Add.

    Note

    While Microsoft 365 Copilot users can add more than 300 references to their notebook, only up to the first 300 will be used for grounding. Copilot Chat users can add up to 50 references in their notebook and all 50 are used for grounding. Supported file types include .docx, .pptx, .xlsx, .pdf, .loop, .page, and OneNote pages. #### About references in a shared notebook

When you share a notebook, recipients are invited to all linked files within it unless there are files outside of their access like premium sources. This ensures recipients receive grounded answers from Copilot from the very first prompt. This process, however, requires that you have permission to share each linked file. If those permissions are missing, recipients will need to request access to the files individually.

Microsoft 365 Copilot users have access to premium sources in Notebooks like Teams, SharePoint, OneDrive content, internal URL, etc.

Copilot Chat users will have access to standard sources only including PDF, Word, Excel, PowerPoint, Copilot Pages, images, audio, external URL, etc. #### Provide feedback

To share feedback about this experience, select Settings and more [Image: Three dots] in the lower left corner next to your name, then select Feedback. #### More ways to work with Copilot Notebooks

Capture conversations and ideas on the go in Microsoft 365 Copilot Notebooks

Important work doesn’t always happen in documents or recorded meetings, but often takes place during in-person conversations, whiteboard sessions, quick brainstorms, or when you’re capturing notes on the go.

Capture lets you bring those offline moments into Copilot Notebooks so they’re saved alongside your project’s content. Once captured, you can review them later, ask Copilot questions, or use them as part of your ongoing work in the same notebook to create presentations, documents, mind maps, audio overviews, and more.

[Image: IPhone-capture-screen-2.]

Note

A Microsoft 365 Copilot license is required to use Copilot Notebooks, and an active SharePoint or OneDrive license is required to create notebooks. Copilot Notebooks is also available to Microsoft 365 Personal, Family, and Premium subscribers; however, the Capture feature is available only to commercial Microsoft 365 users with work or school (Entra ID) accounts.

The Capture feature is rolling out in phases and will first be available in OneNote for iPhone. If you don’t see Capture, it may not be available to you yet. Availability to other endpoints will expand over time.

Learn more about Microsoft 365 Copilot Notebooks in OneNote, Microsoft 365 Copilot licensing, and Microsoft 365 Copilot plans. #### How to use Capture on iPhone

  1. Open the OneNote app on your iPhone.

    Note

    Add the OneNote app to your iPhone.

  2. Go to Copilot Notebooks.

  3. On the notebook list page, tap Capture.

  4. Use Capture to transcribe the discussion, take photos, and type notes during the session.

  5. When you’re done, tap End.

  6. Choose the notebook where you want to save your capture.

Copilot creates a structured Copilot Page with your transcript, photos, notes, and key insights, and saves it in your selected notebook for you to access from any Copilot Notebooks endpoint. #### Scenarios to try

  • In-person conversations – capture key points, decisions, and follow-ups from hallway chats or quick syncs.
  • Whiteboard sessions – take a photo of the whiteboard, add notes, and transcribe the discussion to revisit later.
  • Voice notes and ideas – capture quick thoughts and ideas and keep them with the rest of your notebook content. #### Use Capture on Windows for offline or third-party meetings

Note

Capture on Windows for offline and third-party meetings is currently only available to Office Insiders Beta customers.

  1. Join the Zoom, Webex, Google Meet, or browser-based meeting on your Windows device.
  2. When Capture detects the meeting, look for the prompt in the bottom-right corner of your screen and select Take notes.
  3. Capture starts transcribing the meeting so you can capture the discussion as it happens. You can also select Pause at any time to temporarily pause the capture.
  4. When you’re finished capturing the conversation, select Generate. Note: #### Privacy and your audio content

Copilot Notebooks and Microsoft 365 are built on Microsoft’s comprehensive approach to security, compliance, and privacy. Capture uses Microsoft 365 enterprise data, privacy, and permission controls.

As with all AI-generated content, review Copilot’s output before you share it or take action on it. #### Audio processing

To generate real time transcription, audio is processed as a continuous stream using internal buffering and incremental processing. The audio is handled transiently to generate the corresponding transcription and isn’t retained after transcription is complete. #### Speaker separation

During transcription, audio is temporarily used for speaker separation, meaning to annotate the transcription with information about which speakers made each statement, e.g., Speaker 1, Speaker 2, Speaker 3. Data used for speaker separation is deleted immediately after transcription. Voice separation data can’t be used to identify individuals. #### Data retention

Audio data processed during a Capture session is deleted immediately after transcription and therefore can’t be used for any other purpose. #### Obtain consent

You’re responsible for obtaining consent from all participants before using Capture to transcribe a meeting. By starting a Capture session, you agree that you’ve obtained adequate consent from every person to record audio and photos in accordance with applicable law.

Note

If you’re the IT admin for your organization, you can use Capture in Copilot Notebooks for mobile devices Cloud Policy to manage this feature. #### Feedback

To send feedback from OneNote on iOS, select your profile in the top left, then tap Help & Feedback. #### More ways to work with Copilot Notebooks

  • What are Microsoft 365 Copilot Notebooks in OneNote?
  • Get started with Microsoft 365 Copilot Notebooks

Create mind maps in Copilot Notebooks

Explore your notebook’s content and see how key themes, concepts, or topics connect visually through an interactive mind map. Copilot generates a map of key topics and relationships from your notebook, helping you understand how information fits together at a glance. See connections, view quick summaries of individual nodes, and ask Copilot to help explain a concept further. Mind maps help you make sense of your complex work.

Note

Copilot Notebooks is available to Microsoft 365 Copilot, Copilot Chat, Personal, Family, and Premium subscribers. Creating notebooks also requires an active SharePoint or OneDrive license. However, Mind maps is exclusive to Microsoft 365 Copilot and Copilot Chat licensed users. Learn more about Microsoft 365 Copilot licensing, and Microsoft 365 Copilot plans. #### Create a mind map

  1. Open an existing Copilot Notebook in the Microsoft 365 Copilot app or OneNote and go to Create.
  2. Select Mind map from the quick create with Copilot options.
  3. The mind map will generate automatically.

[Image: Screenshot of the Create menu with Mind map as an available option.]

Navigate the mind map

Expand and collapse

  1. Click the number on any node to expand its branches.
  2. Use the Expand and Collapse buttons at the bottom of the map to reveal and hide all branches.

[Image: Screenshot of a mind map with expanded nodes and visible branches.] #### Get more details on a topic

  1. Click any node to see a summary.
  2. Select Explain to get a deeper breakdown of the topic in the Copilot Chat pane.
  3. Continue chatting with Copilot to learn more and ask follow-up questions about that node. Copilot will reference content in your notebook to generate responses.

[Image: Screenshot of the topic summary pane with an option to open Copilot Chat for more details.]

Export mind maps

  • Use the Export option to save the map as a PNG.
  • The export takes a screen capture, so expand or collapse nodes before exporting to get the view you want.

Important limitations

  • Mind maps are private to you even in a shared notebook.
  • Mind maps are stored for 30 days from the date of creation; the expiration date is shown at the top of the map.
  • Mind maps are available on desktop and web only in Copilot Notebooks in the Microsoft 365 Copilot app and OneNote.

Feedback

Use the thumbs up / thumbs down buttons on the map to submit feedback.

Copilot will provide an answer grounded on the references in your notebook. Learn more about Copilot grounding.

  1. Select the notebook you want to work on.

Links to supported file types within your Microsoft 365 account can be added and used for grounding. Other links, including web links, aren’t supported.

Even if you share your notebook with others, your conversations with Copilot remain private.

  • Plan: Copilot Chat; References you can add: Up to 50; References used for grounding: All 50
  • Plan: Microsoft 365 Copilot; References you can add: More than 300; References used for grounding: First 300

Open Copilot Notebooks #### What is Copilot Notebooks

[Embedded video omitted]

Introducing Copilot Notebooks–your own AI-powered workspace designed for the content that matters most to your task. Bring together all your relevant resources including Copilot Chat, files, pages, meeting notes, links, and more in one place. Share notebooks with your team to collaborate in real time, exchange knowledge, and build together with Copilot. As you work, get focused answers tailored to your notebook’s content and keep adding references that update dynamically as your project evolves.

Note

Copilot Notebooks is available to Microsoft 365 Copilot and Copilot Chat licensed users. Accounts must have a SharePoint or OneDrive license (service plan) to create notebooks. Copilot Notebooks is also available for Microsoft 365 Personal, Family, and Premium subscribers. Learn more about Microsoft 365 Copilot licensing and Microsoft 365 Copilot plans.

Note

Some features described in this article are rolling out gradually and may not yet be available in your experience. #### Why use Copilot Notebooks?

  • Gather your content: Bring together all your Copilot chats, Microsoft 365 files, pages, and meeting notes into a notebook.
  • Get answers: Ask questions and Copilot can analyze notebook content to find answers, identify key themes, and draw insights.
  • Draft smarter: Draft something new with Copilot based on the content in your notebook.
  • Collaborate in real time: Co-create with your team and Copilot using shared content. #### How to use Copilot Notebooks

Create a new notebook

  1. Select Notebooks to expand the list, then select All notebooks. From the page that lists all your notebooks, select New notebook.

    Tip

    To create subsequent notebooks, select the plus button [Image: add-1] next to Notebooks on the left side of the screen.

  2. Create a name for your notebook.

  3. Under Add content to References, Copilot will suggest a list of references you may want to add. You can also add references in a few different ways.

    • Filter – Select All, Files, Meetings, Emails, and Sites to filter on the list of suggested references.
    • Search – In the search bar, enter the name of a specific reference, then select it from the list of suggested references.
    • Upload – Select the Upload button [Image: upload-1] and find the file you want to add.
    • OneDrive – Select the OneDrive icon. In your OneDrive folder, navigate to the file you want, and then select Add.
    • Drag and drop – Drop your file into the section, Drag your files and other content here.

  4. Select Create.

Draft an initial proposal using your collected references: You need a concise proposal to share with stakeholders. Ask:

How Microsoft 365 Copilot Notebooks works

Copilot Notebooks are AI‑powered workspaces where you can bring together your content and sources so Copilot can understand the full picture and give you more relevant, context‑aware responses. Learn more about how to get started with Microsoft 365 Copilot Notebooks.

Note

Copilot Notebooks is available to Microsoft 365 Copilot and Copilot Chat licensed users. Accounts must have a SharePoint or OneDrive license (service plan) to create notebooks. Copilot Notebooks is also available for Microsoft 365 Personal, Family, and Premium subscribers. Learn more about Microsoft 365 Copilot licensing and Microsoft 365 Copilot plans. #### How does Copilot Notebooks work?

Copilot Notebooks operates as an intelligent, scoped workspace. When you create a notebook, you can add references such as Word documents, PowerPoint decks, Excel sheets, Copilot pages, and more. Copilot then uses only this curated content to help you:

  • Receive focused answers tailored to your notebook’s content.
  • Continue adding references that update in real-time as your project evolves.
  • Personalize Copilot’s responses by setting instructions for delivering prompts.
  • Listen to audio overviews for a more engaging way to catch up on your material on the go.
  • Accomplish anything from small tasks to larger projects without ever leaving the Copilot app.

[Image: high-level-overview-of-how-microsoft-365-copilot-notebooks-works] #### High-level workflow of Copilot Notebooks:

  1. Create a new notebook.
  2. Add Microsoft 365 files, Copilot pages, and other types of references.
  3. Ask Copilot questions about the content in your notebook.
  4. Get focused answers from Copilot that are based only on the references you’ve added to your notebook.

You can also personalize Copilot’s behavior by setting instructions (e.g. tone or format) and use audio overviews to catch up on material while on the go. #### What can Copilot Notebooks do?

  • Summarize and analyze: Ask Copilot to extract key points, identify themes, or summarize documents in your notebook. For example:

[Image: Icon depicting a document with sparkles] Summarize the key takeaways from the Q2 strategy deck and meeting notes.

  • Draft content: Generate emails, plans, or reports grounded in your selected materials. For example:

[Image: Icon depicting a document with sparkles] Draft a go-to-market plan using the uploaded product brief and customer feedback.

  • Answer questions: Get focused answers based on your notebook’s content. For example:

[Image: Icon depicting a document with sparkles] What licensing options are mentioned in these documents?

  • Stay in flow: Copilot Notebooks is integrated with Microsoft 365 and accessible from the Copilot app and OneNote, so you can work across tasks without switching apps. #### What are the current limitations of Copilot Notebooks?

  • No image generation or data visualizations: You can’t create charts, graphs, or images directly within the notebook.

  • Limited file grounding: Copilot can use up to 300 files in a notebook to generate answers. When you add a shared location, such as a SharePoint folder or site, Copilot considers the files you have access to in that location and selects up to 300 that are most relevant to your prompt to use for grounding. To make sure a specific file is included, add it directly to your notebook.

  • No general web grounding: Copilot won’t pull in information from the internet—only from your notebook and selected references.

  • Deleting notebooks: Deleting a Copilot page or notebook is currently permanent and can’t be undone. Notebook recovery is coming with a recycle bin that’s in development, and a Copilot Pages recycle bin will become available sooner to restore deleted pages. #### What does Copilot Notebooks have access to?

Copilot doesn’t access your entire OneDrive, email, Teams chats, or the web, and only uses the references you’ve added to the notebook to generate responses. This ensures that all outputs are grounded in the specific context you’ve defined—nothing more, nothing less. You remain in full control of what Copilot sees and uses.

For more information about data, privacy, and security, view the following resources:

  • Summary of governance, lifecycle, and compliance capabilities for Copilot Pages, Copilot Notebooks, and Loop
  • Overview of Copilot Pages, Copilot Notebooks, and Loop workspaces storage and permissions
  • Data, Privacy, and Security for Microsoft 365 Copilot #### What file formats are supported?

Copilot Notebooks supports the following file formats:

  • .docx
  • .pptx
  • .xlsx
  • .pdf
  • .page
  • .loop
  • OneNote pages #### What languages are supported?

Copilot Notebooks is available in the same languages as Microsoft 365 Copilot. Learn more about supported languages for Microsoft Copilot.

The audio overviews feature, for both input and output, is currently limited to English, Spanish, Japanese, French, German, Portuguese, Italian, and Chinese. #### More ways to work with Copilot Notebooks

Provide custom instructions for your Microsoft 365 Copilot Notebook

Last updated: February 2026

When you start a notebook in Microsoft 365 Copilot Notebooks, intelligent suggestions from your work data recommend content based on your notebook’s topic, and you can set instructions for Copilot to follow when delivering prompts.

Note

Copilot Notebooks is available to Microsoft 365 Copilot and Copilot Chat licensed users. Accounts must have a SharePoint or OneDrive license (service plan) to create notebooks. Copilot Notebooks is also available for Microsoft 365 Personal, Family, and Premium subscribers. Learn more about Microsoft 365 Copilot licensing and Microsoft 365 Copilot plans.

Note

Some features described in this article are rolling out gradually and may not yet be available in your experience. #### Provide specific guidelines for Copilot

  1. In the notebook you want to add custom instructions to, select the expand button [Image: ChevronDown] next to your notebook name in the upper corner of the left pane.

  2. Select Copilot instructions.

  3. In the dialog, enter specific instructions for Copilot. You can ask Copilot to format responses how you want, focus on a topic, use a specific tone, and more. For example:

    [Image: Icon depicting a document with sparkles] Always respond in Spanish and bullet points. Make sure to account for branding colors, grey 500 and orange 280. The client prefers their name written in lower case. Write out numbers in words rather than numerals.

  4. Select Save.

You can edit your instructions at any time.

Note

Sharing your notebook lets others join in, view your instructions, and make edits together in real time. #### More ways to work with Copilot Notebooks

Remove a reference from your Microsoft 365 Copilot Notebook

Last updated: April 2026

References help Copilot generate answers based on the content you’ve added to your Copilot notebook. If a reference is no longer relevant, you can remove it at any time to keep your notebook focused and up to date.

Note

Copilot Notebooks is available to Microsoft 365 Copilot and Copilot Chat licensed users. Accounts must have a SharePoint or OneDrive license (service plan) to create notebooks.   1. In the Microsoft 365 Copilot app select Notebooks to expand the list.

    Note

    New to Copilot Notebooks? Learn how to create your first notebook.

  2. Select All notebooks, then open your notebook.

  3. Under References in the left pane, find the reference you want to remove.

  4. Hover over the reference.

  5. Select the More button [Image: Three dots] .

  6. Select Remove.

Note

Removing a reference doesn’t delete the original file or content. It only removes it from your notebook. #### Provide feedback

Share a Microsoft 365 Copilot Notebook

Last updated: June 2026

Share Microsoft 365 Copilot Notebooks to collaborate effortlessly—bring your team into one intelligent workspace where ideas, files, and AI-powered insights come together. Sharing makes it easy to co-create, stay aligned, and accelerate outcomes without switching tools.

Note

Copilot Notebooks is available to Microsoft 365 Copilot and Copilot Chat licensed users. Accounts must have a SharePoint or OneDrive license (service plan) to create notebooks. Copilot Notebooks is also available for Microsoft 365 Personal, Family, and Premium subscribers. Learn more about Microsoft 365 Copilot licensing and Microsoft 365 Copilot plans.

Note

Notebook sharing is not yet available for Microsoft 365 Personal, Family, and Premium subscribers.

  1. Open the notebook you want to share.
  2. In the upper right corner of your notebook, select the Share button.
  3. Enter names or email addresses.
  4. Select Invite.

You’ll see an Invite successful confirmation message with a list of invitees.

In the upper right corner next to the Share button is a people icon [Image: people] . The number beside it shows how many people your notebook is shared with. #### How sharing a notebook affects file access

Sharing a notebook doesn’t just share the notebook, it can also give others access to files inside it. Here’s what you need to know:

Source types by plan

  • Copilot Chat — standard sources (PDF, Word, Excel, PowerPoint, Copilot Pages, images, audio, external URLs)
  • Microsoft 365 Copilot — standard sources plus premium sources (Teams, SharePoint/OneDrive content, internal URLs)

Sharing a notebook

When you share a notebook, recipients are automatically invited to all linked files, so Copilot can provide grounded answers right away. A few things to keep in mind:

  • You must have sharing permissions for each linked file. If you don’t, recipients will need to request access to those files individually.
  • Some linked files may remain restricted if permissions can’t be transferred automatically.
  • Premium sources are only accessible to Microsoft 365 Copilot users.
  • If your notebook includes a OneNote section or page, share the entire OneNote notebook so recipients can access that content. Learn more about how to share a OneNote notebook. #### More ways to work with Copilot Notebooks

Introducing Copilot Notebooks in OneNote, your own AI-powered notebook built right into the notetaking experience you already love. Bring together everything you need for your task or project such as Copilot chats, Word documents, PowerPoint decks, Excel files, and more into a single, focused space. Ask Copilot questions grounded in your collected content and get tailored answers, summaries, and suggestions. As your work evolves, Copilot Notebooks stays in sync, keeping references fresh and insights relevant, so you can stay focused on what matters most.

Copilot Notebooks is available to Microsoft 365 Copilot and Copilot Chat licensed users. Accounts must have a SharePoint or OneDrive license (service plan) to create notebooks.   • Get contextual answers and insights: Ask Copilot questions and receive insights grounded in the content of your notebook. Whether you’re identifying key themes from documents or clarifying next steps from your notes, Copilot Notebooks helps you work smarter.
  • Seamless integration: Copilot Notebooks seamlessly integrates with Microsoft 365, allowing you to bring in content from documents, slide decks, and other files across your account directly into OneNote.
  • Draft smarter: Generate summaries, action items, and drafts based on the content you’ve curated in your notebook. Copilot tailors its responses to what you’ve added, making drafting faster, easier, and more relevant.
  • Audio overview: Easily generate an audio summary of your notebook content to listen to on the go—a great way to quickly grasp all the content you’ve added to your notebook. #### Where to find Copilot Notebooks in OneNote

Microsoft 365 Copilot: Pages

Add Microsoft 365 Copilot Chat responses to multiple Copilot Pages

Copilot Pages is an interactive canvas within Microsoft 365 Copilot Chat that lets you turn Copilot responses into editable, shareable pages. With multipage functionality, you can open any of your existing Copilot pages beside your current Copilot chat.

Note

  • This article applies to Copilot Pages in the Microsoft 365 Copilot app. If you want to learn about Copilot Pages in the Microsoft Copilot app, see Using Copilot Pages with Microsoft Copilot.

  • If you have a work or school (Entra ID) account, Copilot Pages is available to those who have SharePoint or OneDrive storage, including ones without a Microsoft 365 Copilot license.

  • If you have a personal Microsoft account, Copilot Pages is available if you’re a Microsoft 365 Personal, Family, or Premium subscriber. Learn more about Microsoft 365 Copilot licensing and Microsoft 365 Copilot plans. #### Benefits of multipage functionality

  • A fresh chat can be started and still maintain continuity when added to existing pages. No need to revisit old chats.

  • Multiple new pages can be created from a single chat session, which is ideal for covering multiple topics or managing different responses.

  • Pages can easily be browsed and managed through Copilot Pages in the Copilot navigation pane.

  • Pages created in previous chats can be shared, which ensures all team members are viewing the same content and collaborating on it.

  • Multiple sources can be used to synthesize information. This is especially ideal for content creators, analysts, planners, and leaders. #### How to add a response to a new page

Tip

New to Copilot Pages? Get started creating your first page.

Select the pencil button [Image: Pencil icon representing Edit in Pages button in Microsoft 365 Copilot Pages] below Copilot’s response to create a page and open it side-by-side with Copilot Chat. Select the title directly on the page if you want to rename it.

[Image: Side-by-side view of Copilot Chat and Copilot Pages in the Microsoft 365 Copilot app.] #### How to add a response to an existing page

  1. Below Copilot’s response, select the drop-down arrow next to the pencil button [Image: Pencil icon representing Edit in Pages button in Microsoft 365 Copilot Pages] .
  2. Select Add to recent page.
  3. Under Recent Pages, select the page you want.

The response will be added to the bottom of that page.

Tip

Want to see all of your pages? Learn how to find your Microsoft 365 Copilot Pages. #### Provide feedback

  1. To provide feedback on this experience, select Settings and more [Image: Three Dots] in the lower left corner next to your name.
  2. Select Feedback. #### More ways to work with Copilot Pages

Build lightweight apps within Microsoft 365 Copilot Pages

Last updated: April 2026

Microsoft 365 Copilot Pages lets you create, edit, and preview runnable code directly inside a page, which is ideal for quick prototypes or lightweight apps. When you’re ready for advanced features, you can hand off your work to App Builder without losing context or formatting.

Note

  • This article applies to Copilot Pages in the Microsoft 365 Copilot app. If you want to learn about Copilot Pages in the Microsoft Copilot app, see Using Copilot Pages with Microsoft Copilot.
  • If you have a work or school (Entra ID) account, Copilot Pages is available to those who have SharePoint or OneDrive storage, including ones without a Microsoft 365 Copilot license.
  • If you have a personal Microsoft account, Copilot Pages is available if you’re a Microsoft 365 Personal, Family, or Premium subscriber. Learn more about Microsoft 365 Copilot licensing and Microsoft 365 Copilot plans. #### Start in Microsoft 365 Copilot Chat

  1. Sign in to microsoft365.com with your work or school account.

  2. In Copilot Chat, select the Work or Web tab. The best choice depends on your goal:

    • Use the Work tab when your app should use your organization’s data (for example: documents, meetings, or internal workflows).
    • Use the Web tab when you want to explore ideas, generate prototypes, or use general knowledge from the web.

  3. Describe the app you want to build in natural language. Include words like app, interactive, or UI to trigger code generation. For example:

[Image: Icon depicting a document with sparkles] Create an interactive, in-memory-only app to evaluate vendors after each project. The app should allow users to rate vendors on criteria such as delivery time, quality, and communication. Each evaluation should be associated with a specific project and reviewer.

  1. Select Send.

Copilot automatically generates a code-enabled Copilot Page side-by-side. #### Preview and refine your app

  1. Use the Preview toggle to interact with your app.

  2. Continue chatting with Copilot to refine behavior, UI, and logic. Iterate until the app meets your needs. #### Create a full app (optional)

  3. When you’re ready to move beyond a prototype, select Create app.

  4. Continue development in App Builder. #### Tips for a smooth handoff

  • Keep your code modular: Smaller, well-structured snippets transfer more cleanly.
  • Validate before handoff: Run your code on the Copilot page to catch errors early.
  • Use consistent naming: Avoid confusion during import for variables and components. #### More ways to work with Copilot Pages

  • If you have a work or school (Entra ID) account, Copilot Pages is available to those who have SharePoint or OneDrive storage, including ones without a Microsoft 365 Copilot license.

  1. Open the Microsoft 365 Copilot app for iOS or Android on your device.

  2. In Copilot Chat, write a prompt in the Message Copilot compose box, then select the send button.

  3. Select the pencil button [Image: Pencil icon representing Edit in Pages button in Microsoft 365 Copilot Pages] below Copilot’s response to create a page. #### How to view an existing page on your phone

Get started with Microsoft 365 Copilot Pages

[Embedded video omitted]

Microsoft 365 Copilot Pages is an interactive canvas that transforms Copilot responses into editable, shareable content. It provides a centralized place to create, organize, and refine ideas in real time. With Microsoft 365 Copilot Chat and Copilot Pages open side by side, you can guide Copilot through prompts to update or expand your content and ask questions about the page to receive answers based on its current details.

Note

  • This article applies to Copilot Pages in the Microsoft 365 Copilot app. If you want to learn about Copilot Pages in the Microsoft Copilot app, see Using Copilot Pages with Microsoft Copilot.

  • If you have a work or school (Entra ID) account, Copilot Pages is available to those who have SharePoint or OneDrive storage, including ones without a Microsoft 365 Copilot license.

  • If you have a personal Microsoft account, Copilot Pages is available if you’re a Microsoft 365 Personal, Family, or Premium subscriber. Learn more about Microsoft 365 Copilot licensing and Microsoft 365 Copilot plans. #### Why use Copilot Pages

  • Persistent: Save AI-generated content as Copilot pages you can edit, reuse, and build on across projects.

  • Shareable: Share pages through Teams, Outlook, or the Microsoft 365 app so your team stays aligned.

  • Collaborative: Co-create with teammates and Copilot in real time. Copilot creates a page when you need one and updates it from your chat based on your feedback. #### Where to find Copilot Pages

Sign in to microsoft365.com. You should see your name and picture at the bottom left corner of the screen. If you’re creating pages for the first time, read on. If you have previously created pages, look for them in the Microsoft 365 Copilot Library. #### How to use Copilot Pages

  1. In Copilot Chat, write a prompt in the Message Copilot compose box, then select the send button [Image: Arrow icon to send message to Copilot Chat] .

    Tip

    • By explicitly asking Copilot to “Create a page…” or “Generate a page…”, it can automatically create a page when it detects you’re drafting something durable (e.g. a project brief) and keep refining it with you.
    • Add AI tools to your Copilot Chat prompts to get more task-specific and in-depth results from Copilot.

  2. Select the pencil button [Image: Pencil icon representing Edit in Pages button in Microsoft 365 Copilot Pages] below Copilot’s response to create a page and open it side-by-side with Copilot Chat. Select the title directly on the page if you want to rename it.

    [Image: Side-by-side view of Copilot Chat and Copilot Pages in the Microsoft 365 Copilot app.]

Work side-by-side with Copilot Chat to refine your content in real time. Using your prompts, Copilot can update existing content or add new content to your page. You can also ask questions about the page, and Copilot will provide answers based on its current content. At any time, you can make direct edits to your page.

Find your saved pages whenever you need them. They’ll stay available until you choose to delete them. Learn more about page retention in Overview of Loop workspaces storage and permissions. #### Start using Copilot Pages

Here are some examples to inspire you. Copy, paste, and adapt these for your own tasks.

Draft a one page project brief: You want a quick rundown of what a project entails. Ask:

[Image: Icon depicting a document with sparkles] Create a one-page project brief called [Title]. Use these files as sources: /[filename], /[filename]. Include: purpose, scope, success metrics, timeline table with owners, and risks with mitigations. Write in clear, plain language.

Compare vendor options: You want a side-by-side comparison of two vendors. Ask:

[Image: Icon depicting a document with sparkles] Create a comparison table for [Vendor A] vs [Vendor B] using /[filename]. Columns: capabilities, implementation time, security posture, TCO year 1, references. Add a brief recommendation with assumptions and risks.

Draft a customer email: You want teammates to help refine a draft in reference to a support ticket. Ask:

[Image: Icon depicting a document with sparkles] Draft a customer-ready email replying to case [number]. Keep the tone friendly and professional. Summarize the issue, what we did, and the next step with expected timeline. Add a short FAQ section below for internal review.
