How Microsoft 365 Copilot Pages works

Microsoft 365 Copilot Pages is a dynamic, persistent canvas in Copilot chat designed for AI-powered group collaboration. With Copilot Pages, you can turn insightful Copilot responses into something durable with a side-by-side page that you can edit and, when ready, share with your team to collaborate. To learn more, visit Get started with Microsoft 365 Copilot Pages.

Note

  • This article applies to Copilot Pages in the Microsoft 365 Copilot app. If you want to learn about Copilot Pages in the Microsoft Copilot app, see Using Copilot Pages with Microsoft Copilot.
  • If you have a work or school (Entra ID) account, Copilot Pages is available to those who have SharePoint or OneDrive storage, including ones without a Microsoft 365 Copilot license.
  • If you have a personal Microsoft account, Copilot Pages is available if you’re a Microsoft 365 Personal, Family, or Premium subscriber. Learn more about Microsoft 365 Copilot licensing and Microsoft 365 Copilot plans.

Note

Some features described in this article are rolling out gradually and may not yet be available in your experience. #### How does Copilot Pages work?

Copilot Pages simplifies the process of long-form writing, outlining, and research. This feature helps you work with Copilot to transform your chat into new prose, new plans, and new possibilities.

[Image: copilot-pages-high-level-overview-of-how-it-works] #### High-level workflow of Copilot Pages:

  1. Chat with Copilot.
  2. Transfer Copilot’s response to a new page that opens side-by-side with Chat.
  3. Ask Copilot to update existing content or add new content to your page.
  4. Edit your page at any time. #### What can Copilot Pages do?
  • Help you draft content: Start in Copilot Chat to brainstorm, then use Copilot Pages to develop your concepts, gather content, and collaborate with your team—all in one shared space. For example:

[Image: Icon depicting a document with sparkles] Suggest common questions that can be asked to get a retrospective started with my colleagues.

  • Work side-by-side with Copilot Chat to refine your content in real time: Using your prompts, Copilot can update existing content or add new content to your page. You can also ask questions about the page, and Copilot will provide answers based on its current content.

  • Allow you to share pages for collaboration: Your colleagues will have access to the page and its content, allowing them to contribute without accessing your Copilot Chat session.

  • Create a Word document or PowerPoint presentation from your page: Convert a page built in Copilot Pages into Word or PowerPoint, enabling seamless collaboration. #### What are the current limitations of Copilot Pages?

  • Data source scope: Copilot Pages primarily work with content stored in Microsoft 365 (e.g., SharePoint, OneDrive, Loop). External data sources or third-party integrations may not be fully supported yet.

  • Customization constraints: While you can edit and refine AI-generated content, deep customization (e.g., layout changes, advanced formatting) may be limited.

  • AI-powered group collaboration: Real-time co-authoring is supported, but AI responses may not always reflect simultaneous edits by multiple users unless the page is refreshed or re-engaged.

  • Performance with large pages: Pages with a high volume of embedded content or complex structures may experience slower AI response times or rendering issues.

  • Copilot responsiveness: Insightful responses depend on the richness of the underlying data. Sparse or poorly structured content may lead to generic or less helpful suggestions. #### What does Copilot Pages have access to?

Copilot Pages can access content that you have permission to view within Microsoft 365. This typically includes:

  • Files and documents in OneDrive and SharePoint
  • Emails and attachments in Outlook
  • Chats and messages in Teams
  • Loop components and collaborative content
  • Meeting notes, transcripts, and recordings
  • Planner and To Do tasks
  • Calendar events
  • Copilot-generated content from other apps (for example, Word, Excel, PowerPoint)

Copilot and Microsoft 365 are built on Microsoft’s comprehensive approach to security, compliance, and privacy.

For more information about privacy, see the following information:

  • If you’re using Microsoft 365 Copilot in your organization (with your work or school account), see Data, Privacy, and Security for Microsoft 365 Copilot.
  • If you’re using Copilot in Microsoft 365 apps for home (with your personal Microsoft account), see Copilot in Microsoft 365 apps for home: your data and privacy. #### What file formats are supported in Copilot Pages?

Copilot Pages support .page and .loop files. #### What languages are supported in Copilot Pages?

Copilot Pages is available in the same languages as Microsoft 365 Copilot. Learn more about supported languages for Microsoft Copilot. #### More ways to work with Copilot Pages

Identify authors in Microsoft 365 Copilot Pages

Microsoft 365 Copilot Pages makes it easy to collaborate. When multiple contributors, including Copilot, are involved, it’s helpful to know who authored what.

Note

  • This article applies to Copilot Pages in the Microsoft 365 Copilot app. If you want to learn about Copilot Pages in the Microsoft Copilot app, see Using Copilot Pages with Microsoft Copilot.
  • If you have a work or school (Entra ID) account, Copilot Pages is available to those who have SharePoint or OneDrive storage, including ones without a Microsoft 365 Copilot license.
  • If you have a personal Microsoft account, Copilot Pages is available if you’re a Microsoft 365 Personal, Family, or Premium subscriber. Learn more about Microsoft 365 Copilot licensing and Microsoft 365 Copilot plans. #### Identify who wrote what and when

  1. Open the Copilot Page you want to review.

    Tip

    Not sure where to find it? Learn how to locate your Copilot Pages. Need to share your page? Learn how to share your Copilot Pages.

  2. On the page, select anywhere in the text area. If author information is available, you’ll see a profile picture or initials appear next to the content.

    [Image: Copilot Pages identify author and Copilot]

    If multiple people edited the paragraph, you’ll see a profile picture followed by +N, where N represents the number of additional contributors.

  3. Hover over a profile picture to view the contributor’s name and see which parts of the paragraph they authored. Content generated by Copilot will be clearly marked with the Copilot name and icon, along with the name of the person who prompted it.

    [Image: Copilot Pages identify author and Copilot names]

  4. Select the profile picture to see when the edit was made, including the date and time.

    [Image: Copilot Pages identify author and Copilot time stamp]

    If multiple people edited the paragraph, you’ll also be able to see which parts were authored by each person. #### More ways to work with Copilot Pages

Turn raw data into dynamic visuals with Microsoft 365 Copilot Pages

You can build rich artifacts, including charts, tables, complex diagrams, and blocks of code created with Copilot from work or web data. These interactive elements can be added to Microsoft 365 Copilot Pages to further edit and refine with Copilot and colleagues.   • This article applies to Copilot Pages in the Microsoft 365 Copilot app. If you want to learn about Copilot Pages in the Microsoft Copilot app, see Using Copilot Pages with Microsoft Copilot.
  • If you have a work or school (Entra ID) account, Copilot Pages is available to those who have SharePoint or OneDrive storage, including ones without a Microsoft 365 Copilot license.
  • If you have a personal Microsoft account, Copilot Pages is available if you’re a Microsoft 365 Personal, Family, or Premium subscriber. Learn more about Microsoft 365 Copilot licensing and Microsoft 365 Copilot plans. #### Provide your prompt

  1. In Microsoft 365 Copilot Chat, enter a prompt in the Message Copilot compose box. For example, the first part of your prompt could be, “Create a bar chart from . . .”

  2. Select the add icon

    , and then choose Add content.

    Tip

    You can also access your files by entering / after your prompt. Enter the name of the file you want, or select Files and then the file you want to upload.

  3. Select Files, and then select the file you want to upload. Your file will be added to your prompt. For example, the full prompt would be, “Create a bar chart from Quarterly metrics.xlsx.”

    [Image: Chat box with prompt]

  4. Select the send button [Image: Arrow icon to send message to Copilot Chat] . #### Add the element to a new or existing page

Add to a new page

Select the pencil button [Image: Pencil icon representing Edit in Pages button in Microsoft 365 Copilot Pages] below Copilot’s response. This will create a page and open it side-by-side with Chat, copying and formatting the response for you.

[Image: Bar chart example in Copilot Chat and Pages] #### Add to an existing page

  1. Below Copilot’s response, select the drop-down arrow next to the pencil button [Image: Pencil icon representing Edit in Pages button in Microsoft 365 Copilot Pages] .
  2. Select Add to recent page.
  3. Under Recent Pages, select the page you want.

The element will be added to the bottom of that page. #### More ways to work with Copilot Pages

Edit a Copilot prompt to make it your own

Copilot prompts are the instructions or questions you use to tell Copilot what you want it to do. Copilot Prompt Gallery can help you get started, with lots of examples to try or change to suit your needs. This article describes how to use prompt examples in the gallery. #### How do I take a prompt and make it my own?

Prompt Lab provides example prompts that you can edit to make your own. Some editable parts are obvious, denoted by a pair of square brackets, like [topic], [file], and [your title]. But, you can also edit various parts of those prompts, such as the goal, context, expectations, and source, to suit your purpose.

For example, suppose that you want a talking script for an upcoming meeting with a potential customer, and you find a prompt in the Prompt Lab. The only thing is, the prompt you found isn’t quite what you want. You can make it your own by changing the topic, the audience, the type of writing, the length, and the writing style, as shown in the following table:

  • Original prompt: Create a script to explain [concept] to a [role/target audience], including an explanation of what it is, how it works, and a value proposition. Include 2 analogies to help explain it to someone new to this concept.; Edited prompt: Create a course outline to explain Search Engine Optimization (SEO) to marketing interns, including an explanation of what it is, how it works, and best practices. Include 2 analogies to help explain it to someone new to this concept. Include 5 quiz questions at the end of the outline.

More ways to work with prompts

  • Get better results with Copilot

Get started writing prompts in Microsoft 365 Copilot

Last updated: February 2026

Copilot prompts are instructions or questions you use to tell Copilot what you want. Prompts can include four parts: the goal, context, expectations, and source, as described in the following image:

[Image: The visual representation of the prompt framework with examples: goal+context+tone+data]

You can put a little or a lot into a prompt, but all that’s required is a clear goal. If you want to be more specific, add the other parts. You’ll often need to include more than a goal to get the results you want. Here’s an example prompt in Copilot chat, that includes a goal and source:

Write a summary based on all emails from Sam in the past two weeks.

And here’s an example that includes a goal, context, and the expectations:

Draft an outline of a training manual about time management. Our audience is professionals who work in a hybrid environment and constantly need to attend virtual meetings and meet deadlines. The tone of the document will be friendly and suggestive.

Most likely, you’ll follow up on the results with another prompt. Expect some back-and-forth conversation to get the results you’re looking for. #### What can I get done with Copilot prompts?

Copilot is built upon Large Language Models (LLMs) that are connected to your Microsoft 365 apps and data. With Copilot, you can go beyond what you can do with other LLM-powered chatbots by getting data from Microsoft 365 apps and your internal data, such as articles, reports, emails, presentations, and more. With Copilot, you can create or edit content, ask questions, summarize information, and catch up on things.

Catch up:

To catch up on what happened in a meeting, you can ask Copilot in Teams, “What questions were asked during the meeting?” or “What ideas were presented?”

Create:

Want to create a presentation about time management? Try this prompt with Copilot in PowerPoint:

Create a short presentation about time management.

Want to draft a response to an email announcing a project launch? Try this prompt with Copilot in Outlook:

Write an email to congratulate the project lead and team on the launch.

Ask:

Are you planning a trip? You can ask Copilot, “Give me ideas for a 3-day trip in Hawaii.”

Or, if you’re a team leader who wants to get team members engaged, try asking Copilot, “Give me ideas for a team building activity.”

Edit:

In Word, you can ask Copilot to edit a paragraph for you by selecting the paragraph and choosing the Copilot icon to “Rewrite withCopilot.”

Or you can polish a PowerPoint slide with a prompt like, “Add an image of a target with arrows.”

If you have the Microsoft 365 Copilot subscription, Copilot can incorporate your work content, such as chats, email, and files. When you use Copilot with grounding in work data, Copilot unlocks business value by connecting Large Language Models (LLMs) to internal business data. Users with this license can use prompts like:

  • Create a training course outline to onboard partners to Project X.

  • What’s the latest from Sam?

  • Generate a project kick-off presentation based on the topics discussed in the chat. #### A few points to keep in mind

  • Review and verify responses you get from Copilot.Copilot is built upon Large Language Models (LLMs), advanced tools designed to predict and generate text. Occasionally, Copilot responses can include incorrect content, due to the vast and diverse nature of LLMs. Evaluate Copilot’s responses and cross-reference with trusted sources when needed.

  • Using the same prompt multiple times can result in different responses. LLMs are built upon neural network, which introduces some randomness. Even with the same input prompt, most likely, you will get slightly different results each time.

  • Use Copilot in a respectful, ethical, and lawful manner. Avoid using Copilot for any purpose that might cause harm to yourself or others. See our responsible AI (Artificial Intelligence) principles and standards.

Save your favorite prompts to use again

Applies To

Word for Microsoft 365 PowerPoint for Microsoft 365

Saving prompts is an ideal way to streamline your daily tasks. If you frequently use a specific prompt, you can save it for future use, eliminating the need to retype it each time, to help make your workflow easier and more organized. #### Save a prompt you’ve written

  1. Run the prompt you want to save. For example, “Review and summarize today’s emails and provide a list of action items.”
  2. Once the prompt has successfully run, hover over the prompt to reveal the bookmark icon, then click Save prompt. This will add the prompt to the Your saved prompts tab in the Copilot Prompt Lab.
  3. You will also have the option to edit the title of the prompt. You can give it a more descriptive name like “Daily Email Summary and Action Items” to make it easier to identify later. #### Save a prompt written by Microsoft

In the Copilot Prompt Lab, you’ll also find a collection of universal Microsoft-curated prompts. These prompts are visible to everyone in your organization. You can use them as-is by using the bookmark icon or customize them to fit your needs and adding them to your saved prompts. #### View your saved prompts

You can view all the prompts you’ve saved. These are only visible to you, providing a private, customized list for your convenience.

To access your saved prompts:

  1. Open your Copilot Prompt Lab.
  2. Navigate to the Your saved prompts tab.
  3. You’ll see a list of all the prompts you have saved. You can click on any prompt to view, edit, or run it. #### More ways to work with prompts

Share your best prompts with others

Applies To

Word for Microsoft 365 PowerPoint for Microsoft 365

As you get better at using Microsoft 365 Copilot and creating helpful prompts, don’t keep them to yourself. Copilot Prompt Lab makes it easy to find, save, and share prompts that can help you and your organization be more effective.

Note

The feature is available to customers with a Microsoft 365 Copilot (work) license. For information about how to get Copilot see Where can I get Microsoft Copilot?

To share your custom prompt:

  1. Run the prompt you want to share.
  2. After it successfully runs, hover over it and select Copy to copy a link to the prompt to your clipboard.
  3. From there you can paste it into any app you like, such as a Microsoft Teams chat or a OneNote page. #### Tips for sharing your prompt

It can be helpful to give the person you’re sharing with more context. For example:

  • What were you trying to accomplish?

  • How did this prompt help you?

  • What did you learn while creating this prompt or any thoughts on making the prompt even better? For instance, “I got better results using ‘Create an image’ instead of ‘Add an image’.” #### More ways to work with prompts

  • Cooking up a great prompt: Getting the most from Copilot

  • Free online course: Craft effective prompts for Microsoft 365 Copilot

What information does Copilot use to answer my prompt?

You can ask questions, generate content, build agents, and complete tasks in Copilot. To give you responses that are useful and specific to you, Copilot doesn’t rely on its general training alone; it draws on relevant information like the following sources:

  • Your work data - the emails, files, chats, meetings, people, projects, accounts, and opportunities you already work with across Microsoft 365 and from Dynamics 365 (CRM/ERP) apps. This is powered by Work IQ.
  • The web - current, publicly available information on the web. Learn more about web search in Copilot at How web search works in Microsoft 365 Copilot Chat and agents.
  • Content you attach - files, images, links, or text you add to your prompt. Learn more about attaching content to your prompts at Get started with Microsoft 365 Copilot Chat.

Connecting a response to real information like this is called grounding. Grounding is what lets Copilot give you answers anchored in your actual work and the real world, complete with citations you can check.

Note

The separate Work and Web tabs in Copilot have been replaced by the Work IQ button. Instead of switching between work grounding and web grounding, you now use a single on/off control to decide whether Copilot can reference your work data. See Turn Work IQ on or off below. #### What Copilot can access and your privacy

Copilot respects your existing Microsoft 365 permissions. When Work IQ is on, Copilot can only reference work content you’re already authorized to access, and it applies your organization’s security and governance controls to every request.

  • Your data stays yours. Copilot doesn’t train public models on your company’s data, and it doesn’t retain your prompts to train models outside your organization.

  • Your permissions are always enforced. Work IQ works with your organization’s data in place and honors the same access rules you already have. It doesn’t create separate, over-shared copies of your content. #### Limitations and tips of grounding in Copilot

  • If Copilot can’t find relevant, accessible sources, it may give a more general answer. Add a file, link, or specific instructions to ground the response. For example, “Use this file only.”

  • Grounding improves accuracy and relevance and gives you citations to verify, but you should still review the sources and confirm critical details before you share or act on them.

  • Copilot can give outdated answers if you point it at an old website or an earlier version of a file. Make sure you’re linking the latest version, and if several similar files exist, point Copilot at the specific one. You can also ask, “Confirm the publish date and show the exact section.”

  • Availability varies by license, subscription, and admin settings. Not every source is available for every account type, and some capabilities (such as web content) can be turned off by your IT administrator. If you’re not sure which plan you have, check your subscriptions page. #### How does grounding work for different licenses and subscription types?

Copilot can use different information for grounding depending on your license or subscription. Not all types of grounding are available for all account types. Learn more about the different Microsoft Copilot experiences.

Tip

Not sure which subscription you have? Visit your subscriptions page to view the details of your Microsoft plan.

Select your account type to learn how grounding works for you:

  • Work or school account
  • Individual or family account
  • No Microsoft account #### What is Work IQ?

Note

Work IQ is available to users with a Microsoft 365 Copilot add-on license. Learn more about Copilot licensing at How Copilot Chat works with and without a Microsoft 365 Copilot license.

Work IQ is the intelligence layer that helps Copilot and your agents understand your work. It integrates the information in your Microsoft 365 and business apps and turns it into personalized context Copilot can act on, so responses reflect what’s actually going on around you.

Work IQ helps Copilot understand:

  • Who you work with
  • What you’re working on
  • What matters right now
  • How work is progressing
  • What information is relevant to a given task
  • How your organization runs

In short, Work IQ reasons on the data spread across your emails, files, chats, meetings, and connected apps to create into usable intelligence, not just isolated pieces of content. #### How Work IQ improves your Copilot responses

  • Work IQ connects information across your conversations, meetings, files, people, projects, accounts, and opportunities. This enables Copilot to provide rich contextual responses, instead of outputs in an information vacuum.

  • Work IQ helps Copilot understand your priorities, projects, collaborators, preferences, and work patterns. Because of this, Copilot’s responses become more tailored to how you actually work and more relevant over time as it builds a fuller picture of your work context.

    • Learn more about personalization and memory in Copilot at Personalize what Microsoft 365 Copilot remembers.

  • Work IQ can pull together signals from many places to surface risks, opportunities, follow-ups, missing information, and next best actions. Rather than simply summarizing what happened, it helps you answer the more useful question of “What should I do next?” #### Turn Work IQ on or off

You control whether Copilot can reference your work data using the Work IQ button at the top left of the Chat screen in the Microsoft 365 Copilot app.

If you have a Microsoft 365 Copilot add-on license, Work IQ is on by default.

  • Work IQ on: Copilot can reference your work data, your emails, files, chats, meetings, people, and projects across Microsoft 365, alongside the web and anything you attach. Use this when you want responses grounded in your work.
  • Work IQ off: Copilot answers without referencing your work data. It still uses publicly available web information and any content you attach to your prompt. Use this when you want a general answer or are working outside the context of your organization.

You can switch Work IQ on or off at any time, depending on what a given task needs. #### More ways to work with prompts

  • Write a great prompt in Microsoft 365 Copilot
  • How reference and document lengths affect Copilot responses
  • Save your favorite prompts to use again

Write a great prompt in Microsoft 365 Copilot

Last updated: February 2026

Prompts are how you ask Microsoft 365 Copilot to do something for you — like creating, summarizing, editing, or transforming. Think about prompting like having a conversation, using plain but clear language and providing context like you would with an assistant. #### 1. Tell Copilot what you need

Track industry news: “Give me a concise summary of recent news about [Product X].”

Summarize information: “Write a session abstract of this /[presentation].”

Edit text: “Check this product launch rationale for inconsistencies.”

Create engaging content: “Create a value proposition for [Product X].”

Transform documents: “Create an onboarding presentation based on this /[document].”

Catch-up on missed items: “What’s the latest on [Project X].” #### 2. Include the right prompt ingredients

To get the best response, it’s important to focus on some of the key elements below when phrasing your Copilot prompts.

[Image: An infographic showing the four elements of a great prompt: Goal, Context, Source, and Expectations.] #### 3. Keep the conversation going

Following up on your prompts help you collaborate with Copilot to gain more useful, tailored responses.

Generating content ideas Lead with broader requests, then give specific details about the content.

Gaining insights Ask for a summary of a specific file, then ask relevant questions to gain deeper insights.

Enabling insightful meetings Request a meeting recap, then ask for more information about what you should know.

Translating languages Ask Copilot to translate a sentence to one of the supported languages, then ask for more context or a regional dialect.

Storytelling assistance Ask Copilot to write a story, then guide it by giving more specific, relevant details.

Solving technical problems Present a technical problem, then narrow it down, or ask for step-by-step guidance. #### Helpful hints to keep in mind

Know Copilot’s limitations Copilot is limited to your current conversation, so give lots of details.

Be professional Using polite language improves Copilot’s response.

Communicate clearly Pay attention to punctuation, grammar, and capitalization.

Use quotation marks This helps Copilot know what to write, modify, or replace for you.

Start fresh Avoid interrupting and type “new topic” when switching tasks. #### Learn more

Microsoft 365 Copilot: Word

How “Hey Copilot” wake word works in Microsoft 365 Copilot

Last updated: June 2026

“Hey Copilot” voice chat wake word in Microsoft 365 Copilot on Windows devices lets you start a hands-free, voice conversation with Copilot by simply saying “Hey Copilot.” This provides a faster, more intuitive way to chat with Copilot while staying in the flow of work.

Learn more about voice features in Microsoft 365 Copilot at Get started with voice features in Microsoft 365 Copilot. #### How does “Hey Copilot” work?

Starting a voice chat by saying “Hey Copilot” is turned off by default. You need to turn on the Listen for “Hey Copilot” setting in the Microsoft 365 Copilot app.

  1. Open the Microsoft 365 Copilot app on your Windows desktop and sign in with your Microsoft 365 work or school account.
  2. At the bottom of the left-side menu, select … Settings and more.
  3. In the [Image: Settings icon] Settings window, select [Image: Outline of computer window for Quick View settings in Copilot app] Quick View from the left-side menu.
  4. In Quick View settings, turn on the Listen for “Hey Copilot” toggle.

Once you’ve turned on this setting, you can say “Hey Copilot” to start a new voice chat with Copilot on Windows devices, which also opens the voice chat controls.

Note

Language and accent variations may cause accidental activation of a voice chat. #### How do I end a voice chat?

To mute or end a voice chat, select [Image: Unmute microphone button] Mute or [Image: Icon used on the close button in Journal] Dismiss on the voice chat controls. At that point, Copilot stops listening.

If you haven’t spoken to Copilot in 30 seconds, Copilot notifies you that the conversation will end in 30 seconds of continued inactivity. After one minute of inactivity, the voice chat ends automatically.

Note

Voice chats in Copilot may pick up background noise, which can keep the voice chat active even though you aren’t speaking. #### What can “Hey Copilot” do?

Conversations with Copilot are designed to be interactive. You can interrupt, redirect, or end the chat at any time by speaking, muting, or dismissing the session.

  • Prepare and manage your day: Get calendar summaries, triage your inbox, draft replies, and ask for meeting details using voice commands.

  • Brainstorm and create: Share ideas with Copilot, which will ask clarifying questions and help structure your thoughts.

  • Search and explain: Ask Copilot to search the web or your work, explain concepts, translate, or clarify information while multitasking. #### What are the limitations of “Hey Copilot?”

  • Voice chat wake word using “Hey Copilot” is only available in the Microsoft 365 Copilot app on Windows desktop.

  • New chats can’t be activated with “Hey Copilot” when the screen is locked or if there is audio from another app.

Ongoing improvements will expand capabilities and supported platforms over time. #### What does “Hey Copilot” have access to?

“Hey Copilot” only listens for you to say, “Hey Copilot.” It doesn’t listen to any other words or noises. After the voice chat is activated, your voice chat data is handled like any other voice chat in Copilot Chat. No user or Copilot audio is stored.

Learn more about voice chats in Copilot Chat at Get started with voice features in Microsoft 365 Copilot and Frequently asked questions about voice features in Microsoft 365 Copilot.

For more information on data, privacy, and security for Microsoft 365 Copilot, see Data, Privacy, and Security for Microsoft 365 Copilot. #### What languages are supported in “Hey Copilot?”

Voice chat wake word responds only to “Hey Copilot” in English, but chats can continue in any supported language. See Supported languages for Microsoft 365 Copilot. #### More ways to work with Copilot Chat

Microsoft Copilot

About Copilot Health

What is Copilot Health?

Copilot Health is a dedicated space in Copilot where you can talk through private health questions - for example, what your symptoms might mean, what your lab results say, or what kind of care to look for. If you choose, you can also connect Copilot Health to your own data from wearables or health records to get more personalized answers.

Important

Copilot Health is not intended to diagnose or treat medical conditions and it’s not a substitute for professional medical advice. It’s a way for you to be more informed about your health and wellbeing, spot patterns and trends, and get prepared for more productive conversations with your doctor. #### Who is Copilot Health available to?

Copilot Health is now available in preview to Microsoft 365 Personal, Family, and Premium subscribers who are at least 18 years old and reside in the US. Availability is subject to change. #### How do I access Copilot Health?

Copilot Health is currently rolling out on the web at copilot.com on the Copilot app for Windows, and in the Copilot iOS app. If you’re a Microsoft 365 subscriber and signed in with the same account you used to purchase a Microsoft 365 subscription, you are eligible to use Copilot Health. Tap Health to get started and begin your first conversation. Copilot isn’t available on Android yet, but we’re actively working to expand to additional platforms, regions, and languages. #### Where does Copilot Health source external information?

For health questions, Copilot Health prioritizes referencing credible health organizations—selected using principles that have been independently published by the National Academy of Medicine. You may sometimes also see content authored by Harvard Health as a separate source of reliable health information. For finding providers (by specialty, location, insurance, language or gender), Copilot Health rely on carefully selected data partners who regularly update directory services listing healthcare professionals. We do not share your Health Profile and conversations with these partners. #### How can I give feedback about Copilot Health?

Tap the thumbs up or thumbs down icon after any response. If a response seems inaccurate, inappropriate, or raises a safety concern, please use the thumbs down on the specific response and share more information about your concerns if you want to. If you receive a response that is different from what a healthcare provider told you before, please consult a qualified healthcare professional for advice. #### Privacy and personalization

Does Copilot Health have memory?

Yes. Memory refers to information that you have provided Copilot Health which is stored and, if appropriate, used to help personalize responses to your questions. You can always use Copilot Health without memory. #### How can I use Copilot Health without personalization and memory?

Navigate to your Copilot settings, tap Memory, then switch Personalization and memory off. This will disable the storage of information in your Health Profile, which means that responses to your questions will not be as personalized. #### What is my Health Profile?

Your health profile is a high-level summary of your health such as your age, sex, health history or health goals that you’ve requested that Copilot Health save about you. It’s not a formal or complete health record but it helps Copilot Health provide more tailored responses. #### How does Copilot Health use information in my Health Profile?

Copilot Health draws on relevant profile information to personalize its answers. For example, it may consider your age when suggesting appropriate stretches before a run, or your history of migraines for a question about a headache. You can edit or delete anything in your Health Profile anytime. #### How do I edit information in my Health Profile?

To view your Health Profile:

  • Tap Health Profile at the top right of your screen (or the profile icon on your iOS device).
  • From there, you can type to chat with Copilot Health to view or make any changes you want to your profile. For example, you could tell Copilot Health to “Update my fitness goal to moderate activity three times each week.”
  • Copilot Health will update your profile and confirm.
  • You can also edit your profile directly in conversation with Copilot Health. For example, “Save the details about my knee replacement to my Health Profile.” #### How do I delete my Health Profile?

To delete your Health Profile, chat with Copilot Health and direct it to delete your profile. For example, “Delete my health profile.” #### Can I use my prior Copilot conversations to help build my Health Profile?

Yes. When you first start using Copilot Health, you can choose to have Copilot Health review your prior conversations related to your health. You’ll decide what information, if any, you want added to your Health Profile. This is optional and one-time only. #### Are my Copilot Health conversations used to train AI?

No. The information and data you share with Copilot Health is not used to train AI models. #### Is my Copilot Health data used for advertising or commercial use?

No. Microsoft will not use the information you provide to Copilot Health to create an advertising profile of you, to target ads to you, or to otherwise send you marketing or advertising. Your health data is also not sold to third parties such as insurers, employers, or advertisers. #### Can I delete all my health information and conversations?

Yes. You can delete all your health conversations and previously saved health information anytime you want from your settings. To delete all your health data, go to Copilot settings, tap Health, and then tap “Delete my health data”. #### Insights

What are insights?

Insights are the cards you see on your personal homepage when you open Copilot Health. Each one reflects a personalized observation, suggestion, or starting point related to your health. For example, you may see trends in your sleep data if you’ve connected a wearable that tracks your sleep, an option to follow up on a conversation you had earlier, or a small action you could try this week. You can tap an insight to start a conversation about that topic. #### Where do insights come from?

Insights are generated for you from your own data (e.g. information in your Health Profile, connected wearables, or previous conversations). When you’re just getting started, your insights will be more general—typically about common health topics—and grow more personalized as you use Copilot Health. #### How do I give feedback about an insight?

You can dismiss any insights you don’t find helpful or relevant. Copilot Health considers that feedback as it generates new personalized insights for you in the future. #### Is my data used to create insights for anyone else?

No. Insights generated from your data are only for you. Nothing from your conversations, your Health Profile or connected wearables or health records is used to generate insights for anyone else. #### How can I get more personalized insights?

Add information to your Health Profile or connect a data source like a wearable or health record. The more information you choose to share with Copilot Health, the more relevant your insights will be. #### Data security and management

What personal health information does Copilot Health store?

Copilot Health stores your conversation history as well as the details you add to your Health Profile. If you connect your health records or wearable device, that data is not stored in Copilot Health but is accessed in the context of your conversations and for the generation of insights. Please note that your Copilot Health data is stored separately from your other Copilot conversations. #### How is the information I share with Copilot Health protected?

Microsoft employs a variety of security technologies and procedures to help protect your personal data from unauthorized access, use, or disclosure, including encrypting data while in transit and at rest. To learn more about how we protect your personal data and comply with privacy laws around the world, please see the Microsoft Privacy Statement. #### Are my Copilot Health conversations shared with Copilot?

No. Your Copilot Health conversations are not shared with Copilot. #### How long is my information stored in Copilot Health?

By default, we store health conversation activity for 18 months. However, we know your health information and data is sensitive and personal, that’s why we let you control it. You can choose to delete all your health information and conversations anytime from your health settings, or you can view and delete specific information you’ve shared previously conversation-by-conversation, and by deleting specific information you previously added to your Health Profile. Information you delete will not be used to personalize your experience going forward. #### What data sources can I connect to Copilot Health?

Copilot Health supports connections to a growing list of personal health data sources such as electronic health records (EHR) and wearables such as Apple Health. You maintain control of your data at all times. Whenever you want, you can disconnect any wearable and health record.

Important

If you decide to disconnect the data source, Copilot Health will no longer have access to the data except for what you may have discussed in past conversations. #### Can I disconnect data I’ve connected before?

Yes, anytime. From your settings, tap Connectors, then pick what you want disconnected. #### I no longer want to use Copilot Health. How do I delete my account?

You can stop using Copilot Health and delete all your health data anytime you want to by going to Copilot Settings, selecting Health, and tapping “Delete my health data.” Doing so will delete all your health data including your conversations, Health Profile, and connected data sources, as well as result in the withdrawal of your consent for Copilot Health to collect and process your data. If you decide to use Copilot Health again in the future, you can rebuild another Health Profile. #### Why are some of my healthcare providers and associated health records missing?

Copilot Health partners with HealthEx to support automatic, identity-verification-based searches for your health records. You can also manually search for any provider by organization name if you want to connect a health record. Both searches are powered by HealthEx’s network, which includes more than 50,000 U.S. provider organizations. While this network is extensive, it does not currently include all providers. #### Does Copilot Health comply with HIPAA?

Copilot Health is a direct-to-consumer wellness product, and HIPAA does not apply to most direct-to-consumer wellness products. However, the information and data you provide to Copilot Health is protected by Microsoft’s security policies and practices and is not used to train AI models. Microsoft also does not use the data or information you provide for advertising or marketing purposes or sell the data or information you provide to third parties. To learn more about how Microsoft protects your privacy, see the Microsoft Privacy Statement.

Conversation modes in Microsoft Copilot

Depending on the complexity of your question, you may want Microsoft Copilot to invoke AI models that are faster or that can spend more time reasoning over a response. Copilot supports multiple conversation modes:

  • Quick response: Provides straightforward, instantaneous responses.
  • Think Deeper: Takes up to 10 seconds to provide a more thoughtful response.
  • Study and learn: Optimized to explain concepts and guide you to an answer, rather than just providing direct answers.
  • Smart: Use the GPT-5 model to respond. Thinks deeply or quickly depending on the task.
  • Search: Brings you the most up-to-date answers from the web, with citations.

Some conversation modes may only be available when signed in to Copilot. #### How to use conversation modes in Microsoft Copilot

  1. Type a prompt into Copilot. All modes benefit from your prompt being as specific as possible.
  2. Press (or tap) “Quick response” under your prompt and select an appropriate mode to activate it before you submit your prompt.
  3. Think Deeper and Smart may take some additional time to consider and respond to your prompt. During that time, Copilot will share its chain of thought while evaluating your prompt and preparing a response.
  4. After Copilot provides a response, you can continue to the same conversation mode to ask to ask follow-up questions. The selected mode will remain active until you disable it. #### Think Deeper

Think Deeper is a conversation mode in Copilot that can provide more detailed, thoughtful responses. It enhances your interactions with Copilot, allowing for in-depth exploration of complex topics, nuanced perspectives, and detailed problem-solving.

Think Deeper is powered by the latest reasoning models from OpenAI.

Use of Think Deeper in Copilot is not limited, but Microsoft 365 Personal, Family, and Premium users have priority access whenever capacity is limited. Think Deeper works in all supported Copilot regions and languages. #### Example prompts to use with Think Deeper

Here are some prompt ideas to get you started with Think Deeper:

  • I have $15K to use on a home renovation. I’m deciding between a kitchen island, updated bathroom, or replacing the roof. What would increase the value of my home more over the next 3 years?
  • I’m a front-end software engineer with four years of experience. Should I pursue a graduate degree? What are the pros and cons, things I should consider, and impact to my earning potential?
  • I want to make sure I make the most of my last year of college. Can you help me plan what I should or shouldn’t do with my time? I want to get a good job right away but also enjoy my last year with friends and stay in touch with them after I graduate. I need to be ready financially to be out on my own. #### Study and learn

Study and learn mode helps you master new material and deepen your understanding on a topic. It is designed to support active learning by breaking down complex concepts, answering follow-up questions, and providing clear explanations at your pace. You can also ask Copilot to quiz you about certain topics.

Study and learn mode is available to all Copilot users and works in all supported Copilot regions and languages. #### Example prompts to use with study and learn

Here are some prompt ideas to get you started with study and learn mode:

  • Help me figure out the slope and y-intercept of the line y = 2x + 7
  • How does a FOR loop work in Python, and can you show me how to use it to sum all even numbers from 1 to 100?
  • What’s the difference between correlation and causation, and how can I tell if a study is showing one or the other?
  • Can you explain how DNA replication works, step by step?
  • Test my understanding of mitosis and meiosis in Biology.
  • Quiz me on Enlightenment thinkers and their influence on the French Revolution. #### Smart

Smart mode is powered by GPT-5, giving you Copilot’s most advanced reasoning and conversation abilities. It’s designed for high-quality answers, nuanced understanding, and more natural, context-aware interactions. This mode is especially helpful when you need reliable insights, clear explanations, or creative problem-solving.

Smart mode is available to all Copilot users and works in all supported Copilot regions and languages. #### Search

Copilot Search helps you quickly find up-to-date information from the web. It’s designed to bring you relevant, accurate results so you can get answers, check facts, and explore current topics without leaving Copilot. This mode is especially useful when you need timely information that goes beyond what’s already built into Copilot.

Copilot Search is available to all Copilot users and works in all supported Copilot regions and languages.

Copilot Journeys

Browsing the web often spans multiple tabs, sessions, and days. Keeping track of tasks can be overwhelming. Copilot Journeys transform your browser into a smart assistant that helps you resume tasks and move forward efficiently. It automatically organizes your browsing data into meaningful groups called Journeys, each with a quick summary and suggested next step. With Journeys, you can continue where you left off without losing track of your progress. #### How Copilot Journeys works

While you browse, Copilot creates a short summary of your page content that captures the essence of what you view. For example, Copilot might summarize a page with a pizza recipe as a “Step-by-step guide to making pizza from scratch, including dough recipes and topping suggestions”.

That summary, along with related page titles, URLs, searches, and Copilot chats, is used to create a task-focused group called a Copilot Journey. Each Journey reflects a task in progress, so you can pick up quickly where you left off without losing context.

Copilot Journeys are constantly replaced with new ones to remain fresh and relevant. Older Journeys and their underlying data are automatically deleted after 14 days. #### Using Copilot Journeys

This feature is not on by default. To turn it on, enable Copilot new tab page and Journeys in Microsoft Edge by visiting Settings > Copilot and AI > Copilot in Edge > Journeys. You can disable the feature using the same controls.

After you have turned on Journeys, it will work in the background while you browse. After you have some browsing activity, you will see new Journeys appear as cards at the bottom of your Edge new tab page. Up to three Journey cards will appear at a time. Each card displays a quick summary and a suggested next step,

Clicking on a card will open a chat with Microsoft Copilot. There, Copilot will facilitate continuing the same task you were working on before by summarizing information, comparing options, building a plan, and so on. #### Getting started quickly with Journeys

If you’re new to Microsoft Edge or just signed in, you may not have enough browsing history yet for Journeys to organize. There are two quick paths to get started. #### Import browsing history from another browser

If you’re new to Microsoft Edge and have limited browsing history, Journeys may prompt you to import recent history from another browser on your device.

If you choose to import:

  • Only recent browsing history is used to create your first Journeys.
  • Imported history is added to your Edge browsing history and may be used by other Edge features, just like any other browsing data.

You can skip this step and allow Journeys to build from your new browsing activity instead. #### Use your existing Edge browsing history

If you already have browsing history in Microsoft Edge but are new to the Journeys feature, Copilot may offer to use your recent Edge browsing activity to create your first journey cards. This allows you to see relevant journeys without waiting for new browsing sessions to accumulate. #### What data is used

To create your first Journeys, Copilot uses URLs and page titles from your recent browsing history.

  • Copilot does not access the full content of previously visited pages.
  • A multi-layered filtering system is applied to exclude sensitive sites and personal information (see How Copilot Journeys uses your data). #### How often this happens

This is a one-time process. After your initial journeys are created, the feature works on an ongoing basis using your new browsing activity, as described above under How Copilot Journeys works. #### Your choice

You are always in control. During setup, you can choose not to import history or use existing browsing data. If you skip this step, Journeys will generate cards from your new browsing activity as it accumulates. Check back soon for your first journeys to appear. #### Syncing Journeys across devices

When History sync is on, Copilot Journeys data is synced across your signed-in devices so they all generate and display the same Journeys. When History sync is off, Journeys are built locally on each device and will not sync across devices.

To manage your sync preferences, go to Edge Settings > Profiles > Sync. The Journeys sync state is also shown under Edge Settings > Copilot and AI > Copilot in Edge > Journeys. #### How Copilot Journeys uses your data

Copilot Journeys are designed to help you stay organized while keeping your privacy in mind. It only uses the information it needs to build helpful cards for a task. When you enable Journeys, it may access the following:

  • Page metadata and content: The page title, URL, and non-sensitive content from sites you visit.
  • Dwell time: The length of time you spend on a site.  This helps Copilot evaluate which sites may be important to you.
  • Copilot chats: When you use Copilot in Edge, your conversations may be used to create more helpful Journeys.
  • Navigation context: Copilot will consider how your visited sites connect through their links, search paths, and sequential flow.

To help include only safe and useful information, Copilot Journeys use a multi-layered filtering system to ensure generated cards are privacy-safe:

  • Excluded data: When generating summaries, Journeys excludes sensitive sites (for example, banking and healthcare) and personal information (emails, account numbers, IDs, or other information used to sign-in to services), and sensitive topics (medical records or adult content).  Any activity when browsing using InPrivate or Guest Mode is also excluded automatically.
  • Secure AI processing: Non-sensitive content is sent to Microsoft’s secure services to create short summaries and cards. This data is encrypted and securely stored.  It is never used to train AI models or in advertising.  Learn more about the types of data that may be used for advertising. #### How to manage the data used by Copilot Journeys

You can control the data collected by Copilot Journeys in the following ways:

  • Browsing history: To prevent Journeys from using your browsing history, clear your browsing history in Edge Settings.

  • Collected data: To erase everything Copilot Journeys has collected, turn off the feature in Edge Settings. When Journeys is turned off, Edge stops collecting data right away and deletes any information previously stored to create them. #### Availability and sync

  • Microsoft Edge on Windows and Mac, signed in with a personal Microsoft account.

  • Microsoft Edge on iOS and Android; signed in with a personal Microsoft account.

  • Device sync applies to PCs at 5.7.26. Mobile sync is not yet supported.

Copilot Wake Word (“Hey Copilot”)

You can invoke Copilot by saying aloud the phrase “Hey Copilot”. This opt-in feature makes it easy to start a conversation with Copilot Voice whenever the feature is enabled and your PC is unlocked. This hands-free experience helps you stay in your flow when you need answers to a question or just need to bounce an idea off of Copilot.

The “Hey Copilot” wake word is only available when using Copilot on Windows. While it is available worldwide, the “Hey Copilot” wake word is currently only supported in English. After starting a conversation, you can continue speaking in any language supported by Copilot Voice. #### How to use “Hey Copilot”

To use this feature, the following must be true:

  • Your Windows PC is on and unlocked
  • You are connected to the internet
  • The Copilot app is running
  • This feature is enabled in Copilot settings

To enable or disable this feature in your Copilot settings, go to Account > Settings > Listen for ‘Hey, Copilot’ to start a conversation and toggle the switch.

Once enabled, simply start a question with “Hey Copilot” and Copilot Voice will respond. For example, ask “Hey Copilot, what is the weather in Palm Springs this weekend?” or whatever question you have.

Once enabled, simply start a question with “Hey Copilot” and Copilot Voice will respond. For example, ask “Hey Copilot, what is the weather in Palm Springs this weekend?” or whatever question you have.

You can end a conversation with Copilot in any of the following ways:

  • Saying “Goodbye”
  • Tapping the X on the Copilot Voice interface
  • Waiting a few seconds without speaking and the call will end automatically.

The Copilot Voice interface will disappear once the call has ended.

For more information, read more about how to use Copilot Voice. #### How “Hey Copilot” works

Windows will show Copilot in use of the microphone via the Windows System tray when “Hey Copilot” is enabled in Copilot settings and the Copilot app is running (open, minimized, or running in the background). Voice recognition of the wake word is performed locally on your PC. An on-device spotter service listens for the wake word while the app is running. This spotter service uses a 10-second audio buffer in local memory on your PC. This audio buffer is never recorded or stored anywhere.

When the wake word is detected, the Copilot Voice interface will appear and begin to establish a Copilot Voice conversation. In parallel, the audio buffer will send audio from when “Hey Copilot” was first detected to Copilot Voice service in the cloud so it can be used to answer your question. #### Considerations when using “Hey Copilot”

If your PC is offline, Copilot will attempt to establish a connection after you say the wake word, but then it will not succeed, and the call will end. Copilot voice responses require internet access and cloud processing, similar to text-based interactions with Copilot.

While “Hey Copilot” is optimized to work on a broad variety of devices, some users may find impact on their battery when using this feature. This impact can vary depending on battery size, system configuration, and environment. Users should verify they have the latest updates from Windows Update. Some Bluetooth headsets may experience reduced audio quality playback when “Hey Copilot” is enabled.

Users can check with their headset vendor for the latest firmware or drivers which may address the problem. If the issue persists, users can disable “Hey Copilot” and may prefer to use Press to talk to quickly access Copilot Voice instead.

Deep Research is being retired in the Copilot app

What is happening

Deep Research is being retired in the Copilot app for consumers starting August 18, 2026.

If you’ve used Deep Research to create detailed reports and analyses in Copilot, you may have questions about what this change means for you. This page explains what is changing and how to access your existing research content. #### What’s changing?

After August 18, 2026, Deep Research will no longer be available in Copilot.

Customers who use Deep Research today may see changes to how in-depth research experiences are available within Copilot:

  • Microsoft 365 Premium subscribers can continue creating detailed reports and analyses using Researcher in Copilot.
  • Existing saved research content will remain available, as described below. #### Is there an alternative to Deep Research?

Yes. Microsoft 365 Premium subscribers can continue creating detailed reports and analyses using Researcher in Copilot.

Both Deep Research and Researcher help you create detailed reports and analyses. While they are separate experiences, Researcher can continue to be used for in-depth research tasks after Deep Research is retired. #### What happens to my existing research content?

Your existing saved research content will not be deleted as part of this change. #### Microsoft 365 Premium subscribers

You can continue to access your saved research content through Researcher. #### Microsoft 365 Personal and Family subscribers

You can continue to access your previous Deep Research reports through your chat history. #### Can I save or export my reports?

Yes. Existing Deep Research reports can be opened in Microsoft Word and saved for future use. #### What should I do before August 18, 2026?

No action is required.

  • Premium subscribers will be able to access their reports in Researcher.
  • Personal and Family subscribers can access previous reports in chat history.
  • You can also save important reports as Word documents for future reference. #### Does this affect my Microsoft 365 subscription?

No. Deep Research is one feature within the broader Copilot experience. Your Microsoft 365 subscription continues to include the features and benefits available with your plan. #### Need help?

If you need additional assistance, visit Contact Us - Microsoft Support.

File upload in Microsoft Copilot

Note

Information on this page applies to Microsoft Copilot. If you’re using Microsoft 365 Copilot for work, please see File formats supported by Microsoft 365 Copilot.

File upload in Microsoft Copilot allows users to upload images or certain supported file types directly into their chat sessions. Once uploaded, Copilot can analyze and extract information from these files to provide relevant insights, answer questions, or assist with specific tasks.

Files you share with Copilot will be stored securely for up to 18 months and then automatically deleted. Microsoft does not use the content of the files you upload to Copilot for model training. #### Supported file formats

Microsoft Copilot supports the following file formats, including:

  • Documents: PDF, DOCX, XLSX, PPTX
  • Images: PNG, JPEG, PJP, JFIF
  • Text & Markup: TXT, TEXT, JSON, CSV, MD

You can upload up to 20 files in a single Copilot conversation. Files that can be uploaded to are limited to 50MB. #### How to use file upload

Upload a file to Copilot using the following steps:

  1. Press the Open icon (+) and select Add images or files.
  2. Use the file picker to navigate to and select the file you want to upload on your device. Once uploaded, the file will appear as an attachment in your chat session.
  3. Type and submit a prompt that asks a question about the file, e.g. “describe this image” or “what recommendations would you give based on this document?”
  4. You can also ask follow-up questions about the file within the same conversation. #### Example prompts with file upload

Here are some prompt ideas to get you started with File Upload:

  • After uploading a resume: Can you help me write a cover letter I can send to the hiring manager for this entry-level marketing role? Call out my relevant experiences.
  • After uploading a report: Give me a summary of this report and its top three recommendations. Also, provide an APA-style citation for this report I can use in an article I am writing.

Getting started with Copilot in Microsoft Edge

Copilot in Microsoft Edge can help you make decisions, research topics online, and summarize web pages, videos, or PDFs to find exactly what you’re looking for. #### How Copilot in Edge answers your prompts

When you send a prompt to Copilot in Microsoft Edge, we may use multiple sources of information to help answer in a way that is relevant and helpful to you. These sources include the query, recent conversation history, and your browsing context.

Microsoft Edge will determine the sources it uses to respond based on the nature of your prompt. For example:

  • For prompts that need to reference page content, (e.g., asking Copilot to summarize a webpage, video, or PDF), Edge can use information from the current webpage, page title, your open tabs, and your browser history to answer the question.
  • For prompts that don’t need to reference page content, such as “Help me plan a trip to Cannon Beach,” Edge doesn’t use the information outside the URL, page title, your prompt, and previous conversation history to help Copilot respond effectively. #### How Copilot in Edge provides suggestions

When you open Copilot in Microsoft Edge, we use the current web page information, and page title and URLs of open tabs, to suggest useful prompts to get you started. #### How to use Copilot in Edge

  1. Sign in to Edge using your personal Microsoft Account. Learn how to sign in and create multiple profiles in Microsoft Edge if you have not done this before.

  2. Navigate in Edge to a website, video, or PDF document you want to ask questions about.

  3. Press the Copilot icon ( [Image: small image of the Copilot icon.] ) in the upper right corner of Edge to open Copilot in the Edge sidebar.

    Note

    The first time you open Copilot in Edge, Copilot informs or explicitly asks for your permission (depending on region) before using information from Microsoft Edge for the first time. Copilot then remembers this preference and use browser information as appropriate.

  4. You can ask Copilot questions about the page content in multiple ways:

  • Type a prompt. Type in and submit a prompt asking your question. Copilot will look at the page and give a response to your question.
  • Use Copilot Vision. Press the Voice button [Image: Teams microphone icon] to ask a question. Copilot Vision will look at the page and then let you have a natural conversation with Copilot about the content. Read more about Copilot Vision.
  • Ask Copilot for a summary. Select “Create a Summary” or “Expand on this topic” in Copilot if you just want a straightforward analysis of the page content. #### How to change Copilot in Edge privacy settings

If you want to change privacy settings for these Copilot features in Edge, you can update your preferences by following these steps:

  • In the Edge sidebar, select the More menu (…) and then select Settings > Privacy.
  • To prevent Copilot from using the current webpage, page title, your open tabs, or your browser history to provide responses, turn off Context Clues. Learn how to turn off this setting at How Context Clues work in Copilot in Edge.

For IT administrators that need to manage this feature in your organization, you can learn how Copilot in Edge works in your organization.

Getting started with Copilot on macOS

If the Copilot app is not already on your Mac, read how to install Copilot on macOS.

The Copilot app for macOS generally supports the same features available on copilot.com, with the following additions:

  • MacOS shortcut key
  • Light and dark mode
  • Device context

Features not currently supported in Copilot for macOS include:

  • Copilot Pages
  • Deep Research #### MacOS Shortcut Key

You can bring up Copilot at any time in Windows by pressing Option + Spacebar. A smaller version of the Copilot interface will appear allowing you to type in a prompt.

To change the Copilot app shortcut key, go to Account > Keyboard Shortcut and type in a different shortcut combination. You can also delete the shortcut key to remove it altogether. #### Light and Dark Mode

By default, the theme of the Copilot app will automatically match your macOS appearance settings. You can change this behavior by going to Account > App Appearance and changing from Auto to Day or Night. #### Device context

Copilot can help answer questions about how to use your Mac. Ask questions like “What shortcut key do I use to take a screenshot?” and Copilot will provide an answer tailored to your current OS.

Getting started with Copilot on Windows

If the Copilot app is not already on your Windows PC, go to how to install Copilot on Windows. On new Windows 11 PCs, the Copilot app should already be installed by default. You can find the Copilot app pinned to the taskbar or on the Start menu.

Once installed, open the Copilot app and sign in using your personal Microsoft Account. Signing into Copilot gives you access to your chat history, image creation, longer conversations, voice interactions, and other Copilot features. #### Features

The Copilot app for Windows generally supports all the same features available on copilot.com.

The Copilot app for Windows also supports some additional features not available elsewhere, including:

  • Windows shortcut key
  • Wake word (“Hey Copilot”)
  • Copilot Vision
  • File search
  • Take a screenshot
  • View web content
  • Windows Settings support

See the sections below for more information about these features. #### Windows Shortcut Key

In Windows, you can launch the Copilot app at any time in one of the following ways:

  • Press the Copilot key on your keyboard.
  • Using the Windows key + C keyboard shortcut.

The Windows shortcut key can either launch the full application or just a smaller ‘quick view’. You can configure how these shortcut keys behave by going to Account > Settings > Copilot Keyboard Shortcuts. #### Wake word (“Hey Copilot”)

You can invoke Copilot by saying aloud the phrase “Hey Copilot”. This opt-in, hands-free experience helps you stay in your flow when you need answers to a question or just need to bounce an idea off Copilot.

Learn how to use the Copilot wake word (“Hey Copilot”). #### Copilot Vision

Copilot Vision can see browser windows or apps you have open on your screen and then answer questions or help you do something step by step.

Learn how to use Copilot Vision on Windows. #### File search

Copilot on Windows you can help you find, open and ask questions about the contents of a file on your device (including synced files from OneDrive on your device). File search supports most file types and can read .docx, .xlsx, .pptx, .txt, .pdf, .json files.

For example, you can ask Copilot to:

  • “Find my meal prep file” and it will return a file like mealprep.docx or mealprep_plan.xlsx.
  • “Find my files from last week” and it will return a list of files created last week.
  • “File PDFs on my device” and it will return files of that type.

You can adjust permissions for what Copilot file search can access by going to Account > Settings > File Search and File Read and toggling the setting. #### Take a screenshot

If you want to ask Copilot a question about something on your screen, you can use the screenshot feature to capture and share all or just a part of your screen.

Here’s how you use it:

  1. In the Copilot Composer, select + and then Take a Screenshot.
  2. Use the selection cursor to mark the portion of your screen you want to upload to Copilot.
  3. Once captured, type the question you want to ask Copilot about the screenshot and submit it. #### Viewing Web Content

Open and view web content without leaving your conversation in Copilot on Windows. When you click to open a link, Copilot opens the content in a side pane next to your conversation instead of a separate browser window, so you don’t lose context. With your permission, Copilot will also have access to the context of the tabs you open in that conversation (and only in that conversation), so you can ask clarifying questions, summarize information across tabs, or ask Copilot’s help in drafting exactly the right words needed for the task.

Tabs you open will be saved with the conversation, so you can return to them when you come back to that conversation. Additionally, if you choose to enable it, you can sync passwords and form data so it’s easier to work within Copilot. To learn more about browsing history collection and how to control it, see Microsoft Copilot privacy controls. #### Windows Settings support

Copilot on Windows can help you adjust your Windows PC settings. Ask questions like “Make my screen easier to read” or “Help me focus by reducing distractions,” and Copilot will guide you to the relevant option in Windows Settings.

Getting started with Microsoft Copilot

[Image: Microsoft Copilot logo]

Microsoft Copilot is here to give advice, inspiration, and help when you need a hand. You can start a conversation to get clear answers to tough questions, turn ideas into images, or polish your writing until it shines. #### How to get Microsoft Copilot on your device

You can access Copilot in the following ways: #### Windows

On new Windows 11 PCs, the Copilot app should already be installed by default. You can find the Copilot app pinned to the taskbar or on the Start menu.

If the Copilot app is not already installed on your device, you can get it for free from the Microsoft Store.

If needed, you can remove the Copilot app from Windows using the following steps:

  • Windows 11: Open Settings > Apps > Installed apps. Search for Copilot, select the … (More options) menu next to the app, and then select Uninstall to remove the app.
  • Windows 10: Open Settings > Apps. Search for Copilot, then select Uninstall to remove the app.
  • For IT administrators who need to manage the Copilot app for Windows in your organization, you can remove the Copilot app using a PowerShell script. #### MacOS

You can get the Copilot app for free from the Apple App Store.

Copilot requires macOS 14.0 or later and a Mac with an Apple M1 chip or later. Copilot for macOS is currently only available in the US, UK, and Canada, but it will roll out to other markets soon. #### iOS or iPadOS

Copilot can be installed from the Apple App Store.

On an iPhone, the Copilot app requires iOS 16.0 or later. On an iPad, the Copilot app requires iPadOS 16.0 or later. #### Android

Copilot can be installed from the Google Play Store.

Copilot for Android works on Android phones, tablets, and Chromebooks. #### Web browser

Access Copilot using any modern browser by navigating to copilot.com. #### Other entry points

Some Copilot features are also accessible within Microsoft 365 apps, Microsoft Edge, and GroupMe. #### Signing into Microsoft Copilot

You can sign in to copilot.com, the Copilot mobile app, or Copilot on macOS with a personal Microsoft Account, Apple ID, or Google Account. Signing into the Copilot app on Windows requires using a personal Microsoft Account.

If you do not already have a Microsoft Account, learn how to create one.

Signing into Copilot gives you access to your chat history, more image creation, longer conversations, longer usage of voice, and other Copilot features. #### What is the difference between a Microsoft Account (MSA) and a Microsoft Entra ID, and how do they impact sign-in?

  • Microsoft Account (MSA): This is a personal account that you use to access Microsoft services like Outlook, OneDrive, Xbox Live, and more. It’s typically used for personal and consumer services and linked to your personal email. If you do not already have a Microsoft Account, learn how to create a new one.
  • Microsoft Entra ID: This is a cloud-based identity and access management service used by organizations and institutions to manage access to resources like Microsoft 365, Azure, and other enterprise applications. It’s designed for business and enterprise use typically associated with your work or school email.

Using the appropriate account type ensures that you have access to the correct set of features and services tailored to either personal or enterprise use. #### Learn more

Transparency Note for Microsoft Copilot

Microsoft Copilot for Young People

Note

This article covers age limits and parental controls topics related to the use of Microsoft Copilot when signed in with a personal Microsoft Account. It doesn’t apply to Microsoft 365 Copilot. For more information, read Microsoft 365 Copilot Chat for Students 13+.

Your safety and privacy matter. When you use Microsoft Copilot, we want you to feel confident and informed. This page is designed to help young people understand how Copilot works, what protections are in place, and how to use it responsibly. It also includes tips your parents and guardians may find helpful. #### What is Artificial Intelligence (AI)?

Artificial Intelligence, or AI, is a machine-based application that makes predictions, recommendations, decisions, generates content, or takes actions based on goals set by people. Think of it as a smart software that can learn, solve problems, understand what you say or type, and make sense of images or sounds. Copilot is an AI that can do things like respond to your voice instructions, analyze information, write text, create images, and a lot more. #### Can you use Copilot?

You must be at least 13 years old to use Copilot, and in some countries, the minimum age may be higher depending on regional requirement. Teens can use Copilot in a variety of ways. For example, they can use Copilot to ask questions, get help with homework, summarize information, brainstorm ideas, create images, write stories, or learn new things.

For young people, Copilot may use data differently or limit certain features. For example, Copilot doesn’t train AI models on young people’s data, and features like personalization aren’t available. #### How can you use Copilot safely?

Copilot can be helpful, but it’s not a person. Copilot doesn’t have feelings, life experience, or human judgement. It can sometimes get things wrong, make mistakes, or misunderstand what you mean. When Copilot provides information, it’s designed to use high-quality sources and include citations so you can check where the information came from.

Copilot includes extra protections for young people, but it’s still important to remember that AI is a tool, not a friend or a substitute for receiving advice from a trusted adult. This is why Copilot reminds you that it’s an AI and encourages you to check for mistakes.

If you’re ever unsure about something Copilot generates, or you’re dealing with sensitive or important information, talk to a trusted adult first. This could be a parent, guardian, teacher, or another adult you rely on. They can help you think things through and provide emotional support in a way an AI can’t.

Remember: AI isn’t a substitute for seeking help from trusted adults in your life. #### How does Copilot use your information?

Copilot uses what you type to generate a response to your question. For young people, Copilot doesn’t personalize results, doesn’t show personalized ads, and doesn’t use your conversations for model training. This means your chats aren’t used to build profiles about you.

While we take steps to protect your data from misuse, there’s always risk in sharing data online. Keep in mind also that some Copilot features, like Groups, may allow other users to see your chats if you’ve shared your conversation or joined a group.

Be cautious about what content you upload to Copilot, and avoid sharing sensitive, confidential, or identifying information like your address, logins and passwords, phone number, school information, or identifying and personal information about other people. #### How does Copilot handle sensitive topics?

Copilot is designed to give safe, reliable answers to everyone, but for younger users it adds extra protections. For certain sensitive topics - such as mental health, substance use, or adult content - Copilot tailors responses to be age‑appropriate, including by offering general guidance, directing you to safer information sources, or encouraging consultation with a trusted adult.

Copilot also has built-in systems that help keep you safe. It checks for certain types of risky or harmful content and is designed to reply in a way that avoids exposing you to inappropriate or unsafe content.

If you encounter unsafe or inappropriate or illegal content, you may also report it here. #### How does Copilot protect your privacy?

Your privacy really matters. When you use Copilot, it analyzes what you say or type in order to answer your questions, help you learn, or get things done. Microsoft works hard to protect this information and use it responsibly. Microsoft keeps this information to make Copilot work properly, keep it safe, and fix problems when something goes wrong.

You’re in control of your Copilot conversations. Copilot doesn’t have access to your photos, messages, or anything else on your device unless you upload it to Copilot. You can delete your chats if you don’t want them saved anymore.

If you’re under 18, your chats aren’t used to train AI models. You may see ads while using Copilot, and these ads are based on what you’re asking about right now, not on other information about you.

To protect yourself and others, avoid sharing personal information like your full name, address, passwords, school name, personal details about other people, or images of yourself or others. If you’re unsure whether something is appropriate to discuss or upload, it’s always a good idea to check with a trusted adult first. #### What does Microsoft do to keep Copilot safe?

Copilot is built using Microsoft’s responsible AI approach, which means the system is designed, tested, and monitored with safety as a priority. Before Copilot is released, it goes through extensive testing by experts who look for risks, including attempts to misuse the system or generate harmful content. Copilot uses safety filters and clear rules to reduce the chance of unsafe, offensive, or misleading content appearing. Every message you send is checked through these safeguards before Copilot responds.
